The UX substrate

What a CivicOS member sees and works with, as the approved requirements and Bob's rulings define it today, and everything a designer needs to build its experience on. It opens with the decisions that are still Bob's to make.

Part 1 · For BobWhat the product is to a member, in plain words; what is still needed from you, each with its background, the choices and a recommendation; and what is settled.
Part 2 · Design inputsFor the UX work: audiences, use cases, the experience step by step, screens and their rules, how heavy each act is, and the words. Each line is marked Fixed by the canon or Open for design.
Part 3 · ReferenceEvery construct with its lifecycle and acts, who may act, what is decided but not yet built. Turn on “Show sources” to see the requirement behind each line.

Part 1 · For Bob

What a member does with CivicOS

A group runs its own copy of CivicOS to investigate how a government is meeting its obligations. Members bring documents in with a record of where each came from. They ask questions and support each answer with passages from those documents. A project concludes a question on the support it accepts, and the concluded question becomes a finding. The group publishes its findings as a case, in editions.

Then the group decides what to do. From the first suspicion, a project can open an action plan: the matters it is about and how well each is shown, the options open to it (a records request, a complaint, a story, testimony, a filing through counsel, and so on), which it chooses and which it declines with a reason, and up to three scenarios with checkpoints the group judges. Choosing an option starts an action, addressed to an office, an outlet or an organisation, never a private person. The assistant may draft what is sent; a member approves it and sends it by their own hand. The group then tracks replies and deadlines and, for a breach, the seven-stage escalation until compliance is restored.

Three things run through all of it. The record always says what is not known and why. It keeps track of who looked where and what they found. And the assistant finds, proposes and drafts, but a person makes every judgement and takes every act that commits the group.

flowchart TB
  subgraph E["1 · Evidence"]

  subgraph J["2 · Judgement"]
    direction LR
    q["Question"] --> bv["Account of support"] --> prj["Project concludes"] --> fnd["Finding"]
  end
  subgraph P["3 · Publication and action"]
    direction LR
    cs["Case, published"] --> det["Determination against a standard"] --> act["Action or filing"] --> mon["Watching the response"]
  end
  leg --> q
  fnd --> cs
  str["Strength and declared bias"] -.->|weigh| q
  ai["Assistant"] -.->|finds and checks; never concludes| leg
  qu["Queue"] -.->|what waits on each member| prj

In the diagrams below, colour shows how far each thing is built: Built Built, with gaps Not built

How much of it has a screen today

The requirements imply 32 screens. In the old interface 8 exist, 13 exist in part, and 11 were never built. Of the 183 acts a member or administrator can take, 115 have no control in the old interface. The redesign starts from the requirements, not from the old screens.

What is still needed from you

These are the decisions about what members see and do that only you can make. Each one opens to the decision in a sentence, why it is yours, the background with an example, what is already fixed, the choices with what each gains and costs, a recommendation, and a few ways you might answer. Take them in any order; the table shows which are quick.

To answer, reply with the number and your ruling, for example “15: as recommended” or “19: option B, but pseudonymous work shows the same strength”. A question is welcome instead of a ruling. Each ruling is recorded, folded into the design documents, and this page is updated in the same change.

A short discussion
  1. 9What does the action plan surface (S11) look like: options, dependencies, deadlines, resources, outcome branches, declined options, and support status on every element? A short discussion Part already settled

    Already settled

    Settled in part by Bob, 2026-09-29 (action-plan rulings 1–10, docs/development/action-design/ACTION-PLAN.md): A plan may open as soon as an inquiry begins, before anything is published or determined, and is not tied to a publication; one publication may give rise to several plans. A plan addresses one or more related matters, each either suspected (an open inquiry) or determined (a noncompliant, compliant or unclear determination), so it may start from a success story or a records campaign as well as a breach. Options are suggested by the assistant, labelled as the machine's, or added by a member, directly or through the assistant; each option is bound to the matters it serves and falls in one of six categories: mitigation, legal, awareness, journalistic, grassroots or other. Lobbying is an option only to enforce or restore an existing requirement. There is no catalogue of response options and no budgets: neither money nor licensed resources are costed, and a plan is not a project-management system. An option left unticked is undecided; declining is an explicit act that records a short reason, so the options not taken stay visible. A plan holds up to three scenarios, each laying the chosen options out over time in phases, with checkpoints and conditions that a member judges; the system reminds once at each checkpoint and never judges. Options show collapsed as their summary, expanding to their detail, and the list sorts by category, date, matter and disposition.

    The decision

    Use cases: UC-126

  2. 12How are the five trust levels (our work, independently verified, meets standards, not yet evaluated, flagged) shown on information from other groups? A short discussion Part already settled

    Already settled

    Settled in part by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-92): As recommended, 'but there's still more to decide later.' The five levels become an origin mark in the same family as 'Machine work', answering who made this: Ours; Another group's (a published edition, signature verified); Accepted by our group (a new reasoned act, attributed, with a reason); 'Not yet evaluated'; 'Flagged' only when a member's recorded evaluation names specific issues, never a machine's. 'Meets standards' is deferred until an evaluator of incoming work exists. The mark is ambient; a hover shows the fact behind it. It is never composed with grades or strength into one trust score.

What is settled

The design as Bob has ruled it, stated as it now stands. Where a ruling left part of a question open, the rest is under What is still needed from you.

Words

What “rung” means to a member

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356): “Rung” is the weight of an act, shown on every button: can be undone, needs a reason, ends something, signed, cannot be undone. The sequence from releasing work to publishing it (release, stand behind, ground, conclude, accept, ratify, publish) is called the path to publication.

What “finding” means to a member

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356): Finding means a concluded question. The queue's class of items that the record noticed and nobody has judged yet is shown to members as Noticed.

A project shows its stage

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356, K362): A project shows one of four stages (forming, investigating, matured, closed) on its home screen. Three are computed from the questions it holds and what it has published, never set by hand; closing is the owner's recorded act with a reason.

The design's questions

1 How is a contradiction presented to a member, and what does resolving one look like (both sides in the record's words; a resolution that records its kind and may promote double-speak into a finding)?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-76, DEC-77, DEC-84): A contradiction is conditional: two assertions that cannot both hold in the same respect, so resolving one means finding the respect in which they differ, finding the side that is wrong, or recording that the conflict is genuine; a candidate a member takes up becomes an inquiry, whose conclusion records its kind (dissolved, corrected, or genuine, with double-speak promoted to a finding and obligation against act routed into conformance). It is shown inline wherever the two points already sit, through a tension mark and a short 'how do these differ' clarifier, with the dedicated contradiction screen only as the escalation; context may fill in facts and recommend judgements, labelled, but never silently decides. Bob ruled the design's thirteen open points the same day (DEC-84), all as recommended and two revised: an undetermined candidate is a lead; a record duty binds the joined members of every project drawing on either side until it is resolved; two projects' different conclusions are plurality unless they cannot both hold in the same respect, when a tension mark asks how they differ; the question's own conclusion resolves it; the machine recommends only which respects may differ; a hypothesised cause is published as 'cause not established'; publication discloses, never blocks; 'one of them is wrong' needs a written reason; a dismissal gives one fixed reason. BOB ruled the design's other five points (K447).

2 How does a knock become part of the record: what does 'pulled' commit a member to, and does the capture name the doorbell as its provenance?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-78, DEC-88): Pulling a knock admits its material as a capture at 'collected' carrying the knock's receipt, and commits the member to nothing more; vouching stays with release. The source is recorded as an unnamed knocker identified by the receipt (never 'unknown'), the pulling member is the capturing actor and never the source, a knocker may prove continuity with a secret they keep, and a source's identity is held as a dated history of disclosures, published only with consent. The note travels with the capture as the knocker's own words; the contact stays in the inbox. The inbox act is regraded reasoned once pulling admits material (DEC-88).

3 Does a project show a stage (forming, investigating, matured, closed) and a work-product readiness ladder, and how?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-79, K362, K364): As recommended: all four stages shown, 'forming', 'investigating' and 'matured' computed and 'closed' the owner's reasoned act, with only earnable readiness rungs shown and each unearned one stated. Bob set the display: coloured bars that build up one per stage reached, disclosing more on hover (with a focus or tap equivalent) and opening on click to what each stage needs next; each bar is named, and 'closed' shows its reason and never reads as a finished stack. Built at the plane (publication R44-R47, T12; routed T13); what each unreached stage needs, and when each was reached, are specified in T14 (publication R49, N346; BOB read DEC-79's display points from State Rules §4.3, K448).

4 What are the five steps of the publication ceremony, and what does the placeholder say until it is built?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-80, DEC-81): The placeholder is retired: the ceremony and its pre-flight are designed and built in the redesign, its five steps re-derived for DEC-72 (step three discloses any unresolved record contradiction and asks consent for any source identity revealed), and an owner signs in the browser with a registered browser-held key producing the same sshsig signature. A published case may rest on a co-attested Grade B capture, each document's grade and co-attestation disclosed; one whose co-attestation failed may be published only by an attributed acknowledgement and is marked 'self-attested only' (DEC-81). Grade A and further co-witnesses are deferred, their options documented in docs/development/GRADE-A-CAPTURE.md (DEC-81 item 4).

5 Does the project workspace show grades and strength, given the legacy UI hides grades there (UI-16 note) while canon says strength is shown rather than hidden?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-82): The project workspace shows strength: each question shows its capture and connection badges (with testimony beside them when an account is part of the answer) and one plain phrase against the project's bar, such as 'Short on connection · bar B/B', 'Undetermined' named with its reason, never one combined score. Every scale follows the measures map Bob approved (letters grade evidence, bars show progress, weights mark acts), docs/development/ux-substrate/measures-map.html, rendered at https://claude.ai/artifact/TfqcXNaJQ86SZzUA8Xn6Ni.

6 What is the one visual treatment and voice of UNDETERMINED, and how does it differ from 'withheld', 'unrated', 'not looked' and 'refused'?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-86): As recommended: 'Undetermined' is one component with a fixed mark and a line that always says 'because…', the same everywhere. 'Withheld', 'Unrated', 'Nobody looked' and 'Refused' each have their own visibly different treatment and one fixed sentence pattern, never shared with each other or with Undetermined. None is dressed as an error, and all are written as named terms (DEC-82). The exact marks and wording are the redesign's, within this rule.

7 How does the rung ladder look and feel so a member senses weight before acting, especially the attested and irreversible rungs?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-87, DEC-88): As recommended, with the principle in Bob's words: friction is kept as low as possible so the tool fades and the work stays in focus, while members still feel the weight of their work and the finality of some steps; cues rich in information show state, relationships, context, progression and the acts available; everything is self-evident to newcomers and intuitive to experienced members; and a heavier act slows the member down, even for a moment. Friction follows the rung: a reversible act happens inline; a reasoned act opens a reason field in place; a terminal or attested act opens a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs; an irreversible act goes only through the ceremony (DEC-80). Every act's button shows its rung's name and weight mark, and an act whose rung is not yet set is treated as reasoned. Bob then gave the 57 acts that had no rung their rungs (26 reversible, 29 reasoned, 2 terminal, DEC-88) and named six judgement calls that carry high friction: choosing a person's attribution, sharing a lead, registering a subject and setting the group's strength bar open the full dialog stating their effect; approving a filing is terminal and has it already; having the assistant work an objective, the lightest of the six, opens its reason field in place with the run's budget and scope beside it. The rung names stay as they are: a heavy consequence is not a new rung.

8 Where does a member record why something is (or is not) significant enough to escalate, given no significance field is allowed?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-89): As recommended: opening an escalation requires a written reason, why this breach is worth pursuing; and a new act, 'decline to escalate', on a live noncompliant determination records in the member's own words why the group is not pursuing it now, reasoned, attributed and dated, and corrected only forward (a later escalation, opened with its own reason, supersedes it, and both stay readable). Both are prose only: no field, value or vocabulary for significance, severity, priority, urgency or rank exists anywhere, so the judgement is kept without becoming a score. Any joined member who may open an escalation may decline one. The changes to the escalation and conformance requirements are owed, for Bob's approval.

9 What does the action plan surface (S11) look like: options, dependencies, deadlines, resources, outcome branches, declined options, and support status on every element?

Settled in part by Bob, 2026-09-29 (action-plan rulings 1–10, docs/development/action-design/ACTION-PLAN.md): A plan may open as soon as an inquiry begins, before anything is published or determined, and is not tied to a publication; one publication may give rise to several plans. A plan addresses one or more related matters, each either suspected (an open inquiry) or determined (a noncompliant, compliant or unclear determination), so it may start from a success story or a records campaign as well as a breach. Options are suggested by the assistant, labelled as the machine's, or added by a member, directly or through the assistant; each option is bound to the matters it serves and falls in one of six categories: mitigation, legal, awareness, journalistic, grassroots or other. Lobbying is an option only to enforce or restore an existing requirement. There is no catalogue of response options and no budgets: neither money nor licensed resources are costed, and a plan is not a project-management system. An option left unticked is undecided; declining is an explicit act that records a short reason, so the options not taken stay visible. A plan holds up to three scenarios, each laying the chosen options out over time in phases, with checkpoints and conditions that a member judges; the system reminds once at each checkpoint and never judges. Options show collapsed as their summary, expanding to their detail, and the list sorts by category, date, matter and disposition.

Still open: what remains is item 9 under What is still needed from you.

10 What does the assistant dialog look like on each surface, and how is 'the machine did the looking, you do the concluding' made felt?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-90): As recommended: the assistant is a panel docked beside the surface it serves, and it never floats. Its text carries the one 'machine work' treatment every machine output shares, with attribution ('the assistant did this, at <member>'s request'); a wizard step highlights the real control and never fills or presses it, and the member performs every act on the real surface. Two refinements: the panel expands (a draggable divider and an 'expand' control let it take most of the window while some of the member's surface stays visible; it shrinks back by itself when a wizard step needs the member to act; its size is remembered, and one click restores the default), and it docks by screen shape, at the side on wide screens and at the bottom on narrow ones, with the member free to switch edges and the choice remembered.

11 What does a newcomer learn first, and how do starter materials and onboarding fit a first session?

Deferred by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-91): Deferred: 'this needs to be deferred until the new UX is in place and stabilized.' Onboarding is designed against the product members will actually use. Meanwhile the measures map already rules that a newcomer meets strength against the bar and 'Undetermined' first, everything else taught at the act (DEC-82).

Comes back when: The redesigned member surfaces are built and have been stable in use (no major layout changes) for a period BOB judges sufficient; or Bob asks.

12 How are the five trust levels (our work, independently verified, meets standards, not yet evaluated, flagged) shown on information from other groups?

Settled in part by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-92): As recommended, 'but there's still more to decide later.' The five levels become an origin mark in the same family as 'Machine work', answering who made this: Ours; Another group's (a published edition, signature verified); Accepted by our group (a new reasoned act, attributed, with a reason); 'Not yet evaluated'; 'Flagged' only when a member's recorded evaluation names specific issues, never a machine's. 'Meets standards' is deferred until an evaluator of incoming work exists. The mark is ambient; a hover shows the fact behind it. It is never composed with grades or strength into one trust score.

Still open: what remains is item 12 under What is still needed from you.

13 How is a member told a capture's grade, and whether it is co-attested, at the moment of capture (DEC-51, DEC-81)? Presentation only.

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-95): The whole grade note is shown once when a capture completes; a capture that completes later and unattended carries the note on the completed capture and its queue item. Bob extended the question to what happens after capture: the system may suggest that a capture not yet released bears on a member's work under six guards (shown where the member works, never as a notification; labelled machine work with its reason; a dismissal remembered; never during a heavy act; switchable off per member; its acceptance rate measured). Still open: a held-captures list with bulk triage was proposed and not ruled.

14 How does the queue avoid nagging (DEC-69) while making sure obligations with clocks are not missed?

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-94): A deadline reminder is the member's own request, never the system's nagging: it is set up when the action is chosen in the action plan, perhaps by a default the member sees and can change at that moment, and any further reminder is one the member accepts, perhaps at the system's suggestion. As a deadline nears, the item's position, colour or wording may change, as appropriate; that is display, not a notification. There remains no way to reach a member who has not opened the product: no email, push or other outside channel. Overdue is new and re-notifies once; 'due within N days' is not new unless the member asked for that reminder. Threshold looks and wording are Design's.

Other rulings that shape what members see

A project's stage and its cases' readiness

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K362, K364): 'closed' stays the owner's recorded act with its judged reason; the other three stages are always computed. Each readiness rung shows by its own evidence; a published case reads Distributed, never Draft ('newspapers don't publish drafts'); Internally checked and Externally compliant read 'not yet evaluated' until the evaluations exist.

build/rulings.md K362, K364

What a member reads when an act is refused

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-83): The standard sentence, followed by the act's own specific next step or alternative, shown to the member, never a hidden field; BOB reads it as the pattern for every refusal that shares a code.

DECISIONS.md DEC-83; K446

Thirteen points on presenting and resolving a contradiction

Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-84): All as recommended, two revised: an undetermined candidate is a lead; a record duty binds every project drawing on either side until resolved; two projects' different conclusions are plurality unless they cannot both hold in the same respect; the question's own conclusion resolves it; the machine recommends only which respects may differ; a stale side moves nothing by itself; a hypothesised cause is published as 'cause not established'; an irreconcilable conclusion is disclosed; publication discloses, never blocks; accepting keeps the machine's reason the machine's; 'one of them is wrong' needs a written reason; a dismissal gives one fixed reason.

DECISIONS.md DEC-84; K450

Part 2 · Design inputs

Who meets it

Everyone who uses CivicOS or reads what it produces, inside and outside the group: what they want, what they already know, the conditions they meet it in, and what they must never see or be able to do.

Group operator (installs the copy)

  • The person who puts the group's own copy of CivicOS into the group's own cloud account, from a page opened in a browser. May or may not become the founder. Fixed installer Purpose, R1; Distribution section 2 (a sovereign instance, not a hosted service)

Goals

  • Get a working copy running in the group's account, proven to serve the signed release, and later update it without touching the group's record or credentials. Fixed installer Purpose, R15, R17-R18, R33
  • Choose the jurisdiction profiles the copy reads its local facts from. Fixed installer R21; instance-setup R13

Already knows

  • Must already hold a paid cloud account with a payment method; the pages state these prerequisites. Fixed installer R7, R23
  • How much technical knowledge can the install page assume? The canon says the audience overall is non-technical but does not say the operator is. Open

Context

  • Device:
    • A browser; the installer is a web page. Fixed installer R1
  • TimePressure:
    • A scoped, short-lived permission is granted for the install and never stored. Fixed installer Purpose
  • Frequency:
    • Once to install; again on each update. Fixed installer R17-R18
  • Stakes:
    • The final panel shows the one-time password and the member and probe credentials ONCE; losing them matters. Fixed installer R16
    • The one-time password becomes the root of trust, whose custody is not modelled (deferred). Fixed DEC-2 (deferred); Membership v2 section 9

Trust concerns

  • Needs proof, not a claim, that each part serves the signed release before the page says so. Fixed installer Purpose, R15, R33
  • Believe in Oakland appears only as publisher and signer of the release; every page names CivicOS and the installing group. Fixed installer R22
  • Installing into an account that already holds a copy is refused until installs are isolated. Fixed installer R24, R32

Must never see or do

  • Never sees the cloud access token or the daemon credential on any page, log or error. Fixed installer R16, R19
  • Never has an existing copy silently overwritten. Fixed installer R5, R32

Open installer; instance-setup; Distribution v0.1

Founder (root of trust)

  • The first person to claim the copy with its one-time password. Holds everything an administrator holds and cannot be voted out by administrators; the hosting account is the remedy. Fixed membership R1, R7; inventory roles

Goals

  • Claim the copy, name the group, add a second administrator, start inviting. Fixed inventory journey (a); membership R1, R11-R13
  • Can deliver a signed case from their own session (today's only live publishing route). Fixed AI Roles section 3 rule 4 (D-421); Publication section 5

Already knows

  • Holds the one-time password handed over by the operator (or is the operator). Fixed installer R16; instance-setup R21 (#boot= pre-fills it)

Context

  • Device:
    • The setup page is served at the copy's root; the canon does not say which device a founder uses. Open
  • TimePressure:
    • Until claimed, the copy is unclaimed and anyone with the password could claim it. Fixed membership R1; inventory group-instance states
  • Frequency:
    • Claim once; set identity rarely. Fixed instance-setup R4, R26
  • Stakes:
    • The group slug is written once and can never change; a group wanting to stay unnamed must pick an unrevealing slug at install. Fixed Publication section 7 point 1; instance-setup R26
    • Full working-corpus export requires this credential and notifies every administrator. Fixed Membership v2 section 8.1

Trust concerns

  • A founder-delivered ratification is recorded as founder-delivered, beside the signer. Fixed AI Roles section 3 rule 4; publication R14, R27
  • DEC-2 is deferred: how a founder learns what losing or leaking the root credential costs is not designed. Open

Must never see or do

  • Cannot act inside a project without participating. Fixed inventory roles; membership R60

Open membership; instance-setup; DEC-2

Administrator (custodian)

  • A member who keeps the roster, capabilities, keys and group identity. A custodian of shared infrastructure, not an authority over the network's content or work. Fixed Design Requirement 1; Membership v2 sections 4-5

Goals

  • Invite members with covers and capabilities, keep at least two administrators, confirm expertise, register signing keys, set the group bar, instance bias, group aspirations, profiles and identity. Fixed membership R5-R14, R21-R27; strength R15; bias R11; intent R9; instance-setup R5-R15

Already knows

  • Sees cover and handle together (only administrators do). Fixed Membership v2 section 3
  • Sees every project and participant list, but sight is never a position in a project. Fixed membership R43, R60

Context

  • Device:
    • Not stated. Open
  • TimePressure:
    • A ballot to add or remove an administrator waits for other administrators; arithmetic is computed by the plane. Fixed membership R5-R8
  • Frequency:
    • Not stated; the canon implies occasional custodial work. Open
  • Stakes:
    • Cover is required and must not invite a legal name; a group under pressure should use covers that do not resolve to civil identities. Fixed Membership v2 section 3
    • At two administrators, removal is impossible; resignation is refused at the floor. Fixed membership R7, R10

Trust concerns

  • Refusals must be comprehensible: the two-administrator floor and ballot arithmetic are shown with the denominator. Fixed UI-KICKOFF 'Who this is for' item 4; Interaction Constructs section B
  • Membership is not a security boundary: interfaces must not imply project privacy is secrecy. Fixed Membership v2 section 2

Must never see or do

  • Cannot publish, ratify, set visibility or answer join requests for a project as administrator; the only project act is rescuing an ownerless project. Fixed membership R41, R45, R51, R60; case-authoring R2
  • A capability they do not hold is absent from the interface, not greyed. Fixed Membership v2 section 5; UI-KICKOFF constraints

Member, newcomer (no civic engagement experience)

  • A community activist, not a technologist; the interface's centre of gravity. Fixed UI-KICKOFF 'Who this is for' item 1
  • The system must be useful to one person with a few hours a week; materials must be understandable by someone with no prior civic engagement experience and usable within one session. Fixed Design Requirements 2 and 11

Goals

  • Follow the path: questioning, exploring, discovering, documenting, impacting. Fixed System Design section 1; Case Making 'The frame'

Already knows

  • May know nothing about grades, strength, rungs or UNDETERMINED; load-bearing state must be named so a newcomer can recognise it and learn its implications. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (load-bearing complexity is per-level)
  • A newcomer meets two things first: strength against the project's bar, and 'Undetermined'; every other mark is taught where it is used, its explanation one hover away. Fixed DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M
  • The onboarding path and starter materials are deferred until the redesigned surfaces are built and stable. Fixed DEC-91

Context

  • Device:
    • Phones are supported, acceptably as a viewing MVP; features may be absent on some devices. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (devices)

  • Frequency:
    • A few hours a week is the design point. Fixed Design Requirement 2
  • Stakes:
    • Personal exposure: communications with officials may be public records; opposition may target participants. Fixed Design Requirement 11 (security practices guide)

Trust concerns

  • The workflow must not nag or second-guess the member; inform once at the act. Fixed DEC-69; System Design section 7
  • The system never puts words in a member's mouth: no prefilled or suggested reasons. Fixed Interaction Constructs section J; AI Roles section 3 rule 1

Knocker (anyone handing the group material)

  • Anyone, with no account, who hands the group material through the doorbell; possibly a whistleblower. Fixed Intake Doctrine section 2a; capture R30

Goals

  • Hand over material safely and know it arrived. Fixed capture R54 (answers knockId, digest, bytes, received)
  • Optionally, show that several knocks come from one person, with a secret they keep, without saying who they are; the doorbell can generate one and show it once on the receipt. Fixed DEC-78 item 3

Already knows

  • Nothing about the group's working record. Fixed Intake Doctrine section 2a

Context

  • Device:
    • Not stated. Open
  • TimePressure:
    • At most 12 knocks per source and 300 per instance in any 10 minutes; the bound is published with its method. Fixed capture R31; Intake Doctrine section 2a
  • Frequency:
    • Rare. Fixed Intake Doctrine section 2a
  • Stakes:
    • The source is counted by a keyed fingerprint of the connecting address, never the address. Fixed capture R31, R56

Trust concerns

  • The record names them as an unnamed knocker identified by the knock's receipt, never as 'unknown'; the member who pulls the material is never recorded as its source. Fixed DEC-78 item 2; Intake Doctrine §2a
  • Their note travels with the material as their own words, never as evidence of its truth; their contact stays in the doorbell inbox and never enters the record or any publication. Fixed DEC-78 item 4
  • The group is never the first to make them more public: an identity detail is published only with their consent to that audience, or where it is already public, cited. Fixed DEC-78 item 5(a)
  • What the knocker is told about what happens next, beyond the receipt and the optional secret, is not designed. Open
  • RATE_IP and RATE_GLOBAL have no member-facing translation yet (D-508). Open

Must never see or do

  • A knock is material, never an act: it never enters the record by knocking. Fixed Intake Doctrine section 2a

Use cases

The full catalogue: every function the Functional Architecture says a group needs, and what the construct designs add. 158 use cases; 22 have no requirement behind them yet, so no module builds them.

Use caseWhoStarts whenEnds withCoveredSource
UC-001 Search the record
Find documents, passages, questions and legs by typing one query language.
memberA member wants something already held.Rows the viewer may see, with facets, a note of what could not be seen, and widening when an AND finds nothing.yesFunctional Architecture cross-cutting 'Search'; Roadmap section 12 (Search)
UC-002 Search outside sources in one query (city data, legal databases, other groups, news)
One question fans out to heterogeneous public sources with provenance and trust on each result.
memberA member does not yet hold the material.Results with provenance, recency and trust signals, accepted into the record.partial
No module holds a standalone fan-out search over outside sources; FIND exists only inside an investigative run.
Functional Architecture 'Search across heterogeneous sources'; AI Roles section 2 (standalone FIND ABSENT)
UC-003 Assemble what is known about a focus area (Context skill)
Surface relevant laws, reports, other groups' work and request status for an area of interest.
member, project ownerA group orients on a new area.An assembled landscape the member reviews.no
No module requirement holds the Context skill.
Functional Architecture Layer 1 skills; Roadmap sections 9-10 (Orient); inventory inFlux ('Discovery ... beyond MVP')

Get candidate standards proposed for a government act, then adopt the right ones.
member, AI runA question needs its legal frame.Labelled proposals kept apart from standards until a member adopts.partial
The proposal store is required; the lookup skill that produces proposals is not a module.

Write down what the member knows before searching, so looking has somewhere to start.
memberA member knows or suspects where something is.A private lead readable by its author; never evidence.yesMEMBER-KNOWLEDGE-DESIGN section 5

UC-008 Read the frontier: what was looked for and what nobody looked at
Tell which absence is true: nobody looked, looked and found nothing, could not tell, partial, present.
memberBefore claiming something is missing, or before preparing a case's searched section.Four levels (internet, documents, content, meaning) with look states and missing causes.yesOBSERVATION-LOG-DESIGN sections 5-6; DEC-86 ('nobody looked' has its own treatment, distinct from Undetermined)

Bring a document into the record with provenance: bytes hashed as they arrive, a receipt, a profile.
memberA member finds a document worth keeping.A collected document with its provenance chain and an earned capture grade (at most B for a direct fetch).yesFunctional Architecture Function 2; Intake Doctrine

Capture sources that do not yield to a simple download.

Unblock a capture that needs credentials, without the secret ever being shown back.
memberA capture request was refused for access.The request retried under a scoped, encrypted, withdrawable credential.yesSOURCE-ACCESS rulings; inventory journey (e)

Give material without an account.
knockerSomeone outside wants the group to have a document.A receipt (knock id, digest, bytes, time); an inbox row; nothing in the record.yesIntake Doctrine section 2a; DEC-78 (a knock has a source: an unnamed knocker identified by its receipt)
UC-013 Move a knock into the record, or discard it

memberDeciding next steps.Kinds and tiers from the profile, undetermined where the profile is silent.yes
Profile action kinds are not yet live in the affordance vocabularies (affordances R26; inFlux).
Functional Architecture Function 3
UC-118 State a deadline with its legal basis
Put a clock on an action; the machine may propose one from a profile deadline.
memberA notification was sent.A pending clock entry carrying its statute.yesOperational Principle 3
UC-119 Record correspondence: sent, received, no response, fee quotes
Keep the ledger of what went out and came back, including records-request stages and fee quotes as evidence.
memberAny exchange with an office.Append-only ledger entries; a reply becomes a responds_to edge.yesCase Making section 2 (records-request lifecycle; fee quote is evidence)
UC-120 Make a records request
Request public records under the law that governs it, and track the round trip.
memberMaterial is not public.A records-request action with its law.yesRoadmap section 1 (CPRA 26-3028); Design Requirement 8 Tier 1
UC-121 Evaluate a government response
Read a response as complied, partial, denied or none, with a reason.
memberA response arrives or a clock passes.An appended evaluation.yesDesign Requirement 7 (Response Evaluation)
UC-122 Advance, decline, suspend or resume an escalation
Move to the next stage when its trigger is met, or record choosing not to now.
memberThe read proposes an edge.History with who, when and why; proposals age visibly.yesDesign Requirement 7 ('mechanical')
UC-123 Ask elected officials and oversight bodies to act (political accountability)
Stage 7: oversight or audit requests, testimony, legislation that restores an existing requirement.
member, government officeResponse evaluation or legal tools fall short.Accountability actions, each stating its purpose; never policy advocacy or candidate support.yesDesign Requirement 7 as amended; Operational Principle 1
UC-124 Media outreach, public comment and testimony
Tier 1 communications to the public and the city, governed by show-your-work and institutional framing.
memberA finding is ready to be spoken about.An action recorded after it is done outside the system.partial
No requirement shapes the communication itself.
Functional Architecture Function 4; Design Requirement 8
UC-125 Discuss across groups (forum, working-on signals)
Share findings and status with the network.
member, partner groupOngoing.Threads and signals.noDesign Requirement 9; Roadmap section 7
UC-126 Build an action plan: options, dependencies, deadlines, declined options
Lay out what the group could do, with support status on every element and the options not taken kept with reasons.
project participant, AI runA nonconformity finding.A plan (S11) that holds candidate steps; hypothetical premises are hunch debt.noCase Making 'THE ACTION PLAN' sections 1-6b; DEC-25 (plan never published, provisional); Bob's action-plan rulings 1-10, 2026-09-29 (docs/development/action-design/ACTION-PLAN.md; the action-plans module awaits approval)
UC-127 Have overdue clocks marked and the next stage proposed
Know when a deadline passed and that a trigger is met, without the plane advancing anything.
memberA clock date passes.Overdue marks; a proposed stage, never advanced.partialFunctional Architecture Function 5 resolution note
UC-128 Record consequences addressed and end the escalation
Close only when compliance is restored and consequences are addressed.
memberA compliant determination after opening.An ended escalation, or a refusal naming what is missing.yesOperational Principle 6
UC-129 Document pressure against supporters as evidence
Record harassment, legal threats or targeting as evidence (Operational Principle 8).
memberA supporter is pressured.Recorded evidence.noOperational Principle 8; Design Requirement 13
UC-130 Install a copy into the group's account
Get a proven copy of the signed release running.
group operatorA group decides to use CivicOS.An unclaimed copy with its address and one-time password shown once.yesDistribution section 5
UC-131 Update the copy
Move to a newer signed release without touching record or credentials.
group operatorA new release.An updated copy, verified.yesinstaller Purpose
UC-132 Claim the copy
Set the founder's password with the one-time password.
founderInstall finished.A claimed copy.yesMembership v2 section 4
UC-133 Name the group and choose its jurisdiction profiles
Set the display name, claim a domain, and choose which profiles the copy reads local facts from.
founder, administratorAfter claim.Identity recorded with history; profiles chosen, none preselected.yesPublication section 7; build/layers.md 'No jurisdiction in the product'
UC-134 Read the copy's health, build and cost
Know which build each part serves and whether the copy is healthy.
administrator, group operatorAfter install or trouble.Reports.yesinstance-setup Purpose
UC-135 Add the second administrator and record who holds hosting access
Reach the two-administrator floor and note who holds the hosting account.
founder, administratorSetup.An invitation; the answer kept.yesDesign Requirement 1 (at least two individuals)
UC-136 Invite a member; enrol from the one-time link
Create a burner invitation with cover and capabilities; the invitee chooses a handle and password.
administrator, inviteeA new person joins.An active member; the link is spent and inert.yesMembership v2 section 6
UC-137 Add or remove an administrator by ballot
Endorse a proposed administrator; vote to remove one with a reason.
administratorGovernance change.A carried or pending ballot with the denominator shown.yesInteraction Constructs section B
UC-138 Change a member's capabilities, revoke or reactivate
Adjust what a member may do; never delete a member.
administratorRole change or trouble.An attributed status or capability change.yesMembership v2 section 5
UC-139 Declare and confirm expertise
Record what a member holds and whether the group has checked it; gates nothing.
member, administratorRouting questions.Two separate claims, append-only.yesMembership v2 section 1.3
UC-140 Register a signing key
Give an enrolled active member a key that attests.
administrator, project ownerA member will sign.A key whose 'attests' is shown truthfully.yesMembership v2 section 6 (D-158); DEC-80 (a browser-held key registered as the owner's attesting key)
UC-141 Decide whether my cover-handle pairing is published
Let known and anonymous members coexist.
member, administratorEnrolment or later.A per-member setting.yesMembership v2 section 3
UC-142 Create a project with an objective
Start a working group around a stated objective; the creator is sole owner.
memberA new line of work.A project, name unique across the instance.yesMembership v2 section 7.1; DEC-79 (a project shows its stage as stacked bars)
UC-143 Invite, join, leave and remove participants
Run a project's roster with recorded reasons.
project owner, project participantStaffing a project.Participation states with history.yesMembership v2 section 7
UC-144 Add or remove owners; rescue an ownerless project
Change ownership by vote; an administrator rescues only when every owner is inactive.
project owner, administratorOwnership change.Recorded decisions with deciders and reasons; divergence at two owners displayed.yesInteraction Constructs section B
UC-145 Make a project discoverable; request to join
Let outsiders see a project's name and ask to join; the owner answers.
project owner, member outside a projectRecruiting.Existence-only sight; a grant invites, never joins.yesMembership v2 section 7.14
UC-146 Fork a project
Start a new project from an existing one.
project participantA disagreement or new direction.A new project.yesDesign Requirement 5 (forks legitimate)
UC-147 Act on a chosen set of items at once
Apply one act to a selection I made, knowing before I act whether it is all-or-nothing, report or per-item.
memberBulk work.The act lands only on the set the member saw; drift reported or refused; per-item reasons.yesInteraction Constructs section S (D-35, DEC-69)
UC-148 Learn the principles and start from the starter kit
Learn values, principles, the publishing template, data-source and legal-tool guides, and security practices in one session.
member, newcomerJoining.A newcomer able to participate.no
Deferred by Bob until the redesigned surfaces are built and stable (DEC-91).
Design Requirement 11; Roadmap section 15 (not yet started)
UC-149 Show that several knocks came from one person, without saying who
Let the group link later material to earlier material from the same source, without revealing identity.
knockerA knocker returns with more.The record shows a pseudonym and states that the same knocker secret was presented, never an identity.noDEC-78 item 3; Intake Doctrine §2a
UC-150 Record how a source's identity became known, and publish it only with consent
Keep what is known of a source as it grows, without the group ever being first to expose them.
member, project ownerSomething about a source's identity becomes known.A dated, attributed history of disclosures; an identity detail published only with consent to that audience or where already public, cited; a firmer identity reaches resting findings as a notice.noDEC-78 item 5
UC-151 See where a project stands: its stage and its cases' readiness
Sense at a glance how far a project has come and what the next stage needs.
member, project ownerA member opens a project.Stage bars and readiness rungs computed from the record, each unreached step stating what it still needs; 'closed' with its reason.yes
The stacked bars are decided (DEC-79), not built in the interface.
State Rules §4.3; K362; K364; DEC-79
UC-152 Publish a case resting on co-attested Grade B copies, each disclosed
Publish without Grade A, stating how checkable each document is.
project ownerA case is ready to publish.The case discloses each document's capture grade and whether it is co-attested.partialDEC-81 item 1; Intake Doctrine §3
UC-153 Publish a document whose co-attestation failed, by an acknowledgement
Keep evidence that can never be co-archived, disclosing its limit rather than dropping it.
project ownerThe pre-flight finds a load-bearing Grade B without co-attestation.The pre-flight retries first; if still missing, an attributed acknowledgement with a reason, and the document marked 'self-attested only'; never refused outright.noDEC-81 item 3
UC-154 Decline to escalate, saying why not now
Keep the group's judgement not to pursue a breach, without turning it into a score.
project participantA live noncompliant determination the group chooses not to pursue now.A reasoned, attributed, dated decline in the member's own words; a later escalation supersedes it and both stay readable.noDEC-89
UC-155 Resolve a conflict inline: say how two things differ, or which is wrong
Settle an apparent conflict where it is met, without leaving the work.
memberA tension mark appears where two points sit together.A stated explanation (resolved when evidenced, otherwise softened), or one side stale with a written reason, or a lead dismissed with a fixed reason.noDEC-77 item 1; DEC-84 (16)-(17)
UC-156 Accept the machine's recommendation in one attributed act
Use a labelled recommendation without it silently becoming the member's decision.
member, AI runA recommendation is shown first, with its reason.The record keeps that the member accepted it, and which one; the machine's reason stays the machine's; the acceptance rate is measured.noDEC-77 item 3(b); DEC-84 (14); K447 point 15
UC-157 Sign in the browser with a browser-held key
Sign a case without command-line tools, producing a signature strangers can verify.
project ownerThe ceremony reaches its last step.The same sshsig signature the plane verifies, confirmed by passphrase or device unlock; a lost key revoked and replaced.noDEC-80 item 4
UC-158 Disclose an unresolved record contradiction when publishing
Publish a finding whose basis holds an unresolved conflict, saying so rather than hiding or blocking.
project ownerThe pre-flight finds an open record tension on the case's basis.An attributed disclosure on the case, both sides with their sources; undisclosed, the case is refused; disclosed, it is not.noDEC-76 item 4; DEC-84 (11)-(13)

The experience, step by step

The long paths through the work, as the person taking each step meets it: what they know at that moment, what they decide, what can go wrong, how long it takes, who they hand it to, and where trust is at risk.

(a) A group sets up and invites members · 10 steps
  1. group operator · One sitting; up to ten self-test tries.
    Install the signed release into the group's account
    Knows
    Needs a paid account with a payment method; the page states prerequisites.
    Decides
    Which slug to use (written once, public forever) and which jurisdiction profiles to offer.
    Can go wrong
    • Existing copy for the slug: refused, nothing changed (installer R5).
    • No payment method: refused, saying nothing was created (installer R4, R7).
    • Account already holds a copy: refused until installs are isolated (installer R32).
    • A part not serving the release: named, not claimed as done (installer R15, R33).
    Hands to
    founder (the one-time password, shown once)
    Trust risk
    The slug is public and permanent; a group wanting anonymity must choose an unrevealing one now.
    Open here
    • How the operator is told, in plain words, that the slug is permanent and public at the moment of choosing.

    Open installer R2-R16, R21-R24, R32-R33; Publication section 7 point 1

  2. founder · Minutes.
    Open the setup page and claim with the one-time password
    Knows
    Holds the password (the link may carry it as #boot=).
    Decides
    Choose a password of at least 12 characters.
    Can go wrong
    • ALREADY_CLAIMED (membership R1).
    • PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT.
    • The page could not read the group: says so, never 'none recorded' (instance-setup R20).
    Hands to
    founder
    Trust risk
    The founder becomes the root of trust with no custody model (DEC-2 deferred).

    Fixed membership R1; instance-setup R20-R22; control-plane R15

  3. founder / administrator · Minutes; domain re-checked daily.
    Set display name, domain and jurisdiction profiles
    Knows
    The slug; the held profiles by name and coverage.
    Decides
    What the group calls itself; which domain to claim; which profiles (none preselected).
    Can go wrong
    • GROUP_DISPLAY_NAME_MALFORMED.
    • Domain verdict absent, mismatched or UNDETERMINED: not shown publicly; members see the state (instance-setup R8-R10).
    • Changing profiles changes how local facts read (warning; instance-setup R15).
    Hands to
    administrators
    Trust risk
    Showing an unverified domain would claim an association the record cannot support.

    Fixed instance-setup R5-R16; Publication section 7 (profiles built T12, N10)

  4. founder · Minutes; then waits for the invitee.
    Add a second administrator (direct while fewer than two)
    Knows
    The floor is two administrators.
    Decides
    Who, with what cover (not a legal name).
    Can go wrong
    • NOT_AN_ADMIN.
    • Cover typed as a legal name defeats the protection (Membership v2 section 3).
    Hands to
    the invitee
    Trust risk
    Cover invites a legal name if the field is worded badly.

    Fixed membership R12-R14; Membership v2 section 3

  5. the group, asked when the second administrator is added · Minutes.
    Record who holds hosting access
    Knows
    Hosting access is outside the app.
    Decides
    Whom to record.
    Can go wrong
    • Left unanswered: the record keeps whatever answer was given.
    Hands to
    administrators
    Trust risk
    None named in canon.
    Open here
    • What the group is told about why this matters (the hosting account is the remedy against the founder).

    Open membership R11

  6. administrator · Minutes per member.
    Add members with capabilities (default contribute)
    Knows
    Capabilities: contribute, publish, create_projects.
    Decides
    Cover, capabilities, declared expertise.
    Can go wrong
    • At two or more administrators, a new administrator is only proposed and needs a ballot (membership R14).
    • BAD_CAPABILITY.
    Hands to
    invitee (burner URL sent by a channel the system does not record)
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed membership R9, R12-R15; Membership v2 section 6

  7. invitee · Minutes; the link is spent on use.
    Open the link, choose handle and password
    Knows
    Sees cover, role, capabilities and expertise; never the member id.
    Decides
    Handle (public in the record) and password.
    Can go wrong
    • NO_SUCH_INVITATION (spent or never existed, one answer).
    • HANDLE_TAKEN.
    • BAD_HANDLE.
    • PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT.
    Hands to
    member (active)
    Trust risk
    The handle is what the public sees; the member may not realise.
    Open here
    • What a newcomer learns first is deferred until the redesign is built and stable (DEC-91); meanwhile strength against the bar and 'Undetermined' come first (DEC-82).

    Open membership R15-R16; Membership v2 section 3

  8. member with create_projects · Minutes.
    Create a project with an objective
    Knows
    A project name is unique across the instance.
    Decides
    Name and objective.
    Can go wrong
    • NAME_TAKEN.
    • A project with no objective is refused (intent R1).
    Hands to
    owner
    Trust risk
    Members may believe projects are private; visibility is organisation, not secrecy.

    Fixed promotion R19; intent R1; membership R71; Membership v2 section 2

  9. owner, then participant · Waits for each invitee.
    Invite participants; they join
    Knows
    Handles.
    Decides
    Whom to invite.
    Can go wrong
    • NOT_THE_OWNER.
    • NO_SUCH_HANDLE.
    • ALREADY_A_PARTICIPANT.
    • NOT_INVITED on join.
    Hands to
    participants
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed membership R32-R34

  10. owner, outside member · Days; requests can lapse.
    Make the project discoverable; an outsider requests to join; owner grants
    Knows
    Outsiders see the name only.
    Decides
    Whether to expose the name; whether to grant.
    Can go wrong
    • PROJECT_SEEN_NOT_A_PARTICIPANT (C-70.1) for acts beyond existence.
    • A grant invites; the requester still has to join (membership R51).
    Hands to
    owner
    Trust risk
    Exposing a name tells every member the project exists.

    Fixed membership R44-R53, R77

(b) From a question to a published case · 16 steps
  1. member · Seconds to minutes; renders may be deferred.
    Capture a public document
    Knows
    An address.
    Decides
    Which route (direct, rendered, archive, Drive).
    Can go wrong
    • BAD_LOCATOR.
    • HOST_COOLING_OFF: the governor paces the host (capture R7).
    • Source refused or fetch failed, recorded as such (capture R8).
    • Rendered content UNDETERMINED; archive capture at most C.
    Hands to
    member (release)
    Trust risk
    A capture is not evidence until a member stands behind it; the grade note may be discarded before the member sees it (DEC-51).

    Fixed capture R2-R18; AI Roles rule 4; DEC-51

  2. member at the release moment · Minutes per batch.
    Release it (vouch for it)
    Knows
    Must see the source material itself, never only an AI summary.
    Decides
    Stand behind it, with acknowledgement and mitigation for a bulk release.
    Can go wrong
    • Refused with offenders named (weight refuse: all or nothing).
    • A second member released first: CAS_STALE (promotion R1).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    Click-through bulk release would make the acknowledgement dishonest.
    Open here
    • No owning module requirement holds release (inFlux).

    Open UI-KICKOFF item 2; Interaction Constructs section J; affordances R1-R2

  3. member · Minutes.
    Resolve its references to subjects
    Knows
    Recognised references with grades.
    Decides
    Accept matches; testify a missed one (grade D).
    Can go wrong
    • Ambiguous alias kept, never resolved silently (entities R6).
    • A C match never reads established.
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    One click from an established resolution off a machine-composed list (DEC-53).

    Fixed entities R9-R12, R27; DEC-53

  4. member or assistant · Minutes; ageing interval set per instance.
    Open a question (or the assistant surfaces one)
    Knows
    What they want to know.
    Decides
    Wording of the question.
    Can go wrong
    • Surfaced by the assistant: must look surfaced; ages to deferred if nobody acts (intent R17).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    A machine question must not read as a member's obligation (D-82).

    Fixed inquiry R1-R2; ai-runs R25-R27; intent R16-R17

  5. member · Ongoing, days.
    Cite documents, passages or questions as supporting or cutting against
    Knows
    Earned grades per target.
    Decides
    Role of each leg.
    Can go wrong
    • RETIRED_NOT_CITABLE.
    • A grouped question refuses a new leg until ungrouped (DEC-50).
    • A newer capture may exist: stale version notice.
    • A supporting and a cutting-against leg that conflict carry a tension mark; the clarifier asks how they differ (decided: DEC-77).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    Stating a grade the record did not earn: the leg is capped and why is shown.

    Fixed citation R1-R8; inquiry R4-R14; DEC-50

  6. member · Minutes to hours; resumable.
    Open an assistant run to check or investigate
    Knows
    The run's bounds, lens and bar are recorded at open.
    Decides
    Mode (only CHECK deployed).
    Can go wrong
    • Mode not deployed: refused (ai-runs R40).
    • A bound stops the run and the log says which.
    • Capture requests wait on the daemon (hours).
    Hands to
    assistant, then member
    Trust risk
    The assistant appearing to conclude; transcripts are notes, never record.

    Fixed ai-runs R9-R24, R40; AI Roles section 3

  7. member · Minutes.
    Accept a version (after the shared-origin check)
    Knows
    Versions with legs and per-axis strength; suggested ones labelled.
    Decides
    Accept, reject, consider, revert; preview first.
    Can go wrong
    • VERSION_* refusals; MACHINE_CANNOT_MOVE_VERSION for a machine.
    • Grounds that share an origin.
    Hands to
    project participant
    Trust risk
    Accepting a machine suggestion without reading it (DEC-68: the record cannot tell).

    Fixed basis-versions R4-R14; strength R11-R12; DEC-68

  8. joined participant · Minutes.
    Make it current for the project
    Knows
    Which version is accepted.
    Decides
    Which account the project stands on.
    Can go wrong
    • VERSION_NOT_ACCEPTED.
    • Not a joined participant.
    Hands to
    joined participant
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed basis-versions R13, R15, R31

  9. joined participant · Minutes.
    Conclude, stating what would falsify it (or that nothing would)
    Knows
    The current version and its strength.
    Decides
    The conclusion and falsifier.
    Can go wrong
    • MACHINE_CANNOT_CONCLUDE.
    • NO_CLAIM.
    • Another project concluded differently: both stances stand.
    Hands to
    owner
    Trust risk
    False certainty: a falsifier of 'none' must be the member's stated override.

    Fixed basis-versions R16-R19; System Design row 8 (UI-64)

  10. owner · Days if more evidence is needed.
    Check strength against the bar; clear hunches
    Knows
    Per-axis pair per load-bearing finding; the project bar.
    Decides
    Which findings are load-bearing; which hunches to clear.
    Can go wrong
    • BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH naming member, axis, required and reached (case-authoring R6).
    • UNCLEARED_HUNCH (not yet enforced; lands with the ceremony's pre-flight, DEC-80 item 3).
    Hands to
    owner
    Trust risk
    Over-claiming: a case over an uncleared hunch states a strength that is not true.

    Fixed case-authoring R6, R12; strength R5; DEC-20

  11. editor / owner · Days (waiting on reviewers).
    Draft a review copy; grant outside reviewers
    Knows
    The copy is complete as a publication can be and names what is missing.
    Decides
    Whom to grant; when to revoke.
    Can go wrong
    • Grant dies if the draft moves case identity (review R8).
    • Recipient sees findings as the last editor could, absent ones by role only.
    Hands to
    review recipient
    Trust risk
    Calling it 'pre-publish' asserts a future that may not happen.

    Fixed review R3-R17; Publication section 6A

  12. owner · Hours.
    Prepare the case: scope, statement, exclusions, bias acknowledgement
    Knows
    Concluded findings; the searched section computed from the log.
    Decides
    Roles, scope, what is left out and why.
    Can go wrong
    • COMPLETENESS_CARRIED_FORWARD (byte-identical to last edition).
    • CASE_SEARCHED_UNCOMPUTABLE.
    • ALREADY_A_CASE_MEMBER.
    • CASE_IDENTITY_AMBIGUOUS.
    Hands to
    members / reviewers
    Trust risk
    Nothing may draft framing for the member (Case Making section 4a).

    Fixed case-authoring R1-R18

  13. joined participant or review recipient (not its writer) · Days; optional.
    Acknowledge the exclusion statement
    Knows
    The statement, shown first.
    Decides
    Acknowledge, or not.
    Can go wrong
    • STATEMENT_ACK_BY_ITS_AUTHOR.
    • STATEMENT_ACK_NOT_A_PARTICIPANT.
    • STATEMENT_ACK_ALREADY_SIGNED.
    • STATEMENT_ACK_AUTHOR_UNDETERMINED.
    Hands to
    owner
    Trust risk
    A gate pushing a one-person group to recruit a signature would be a bug; nobody-but-author is disclosed, not refused.

    Fixed case-authoring R19-R21; Publication section 3 rule 11

  14. owner · Minutes, once the pre-flight is clear.
    Go through the publication ceremony and sign in the browser
    Knows
    Before the first screen, what would stop publication (the pre-flight). Then: what becomes permanent; what it rests on; what it leaves out, including any unresolved record contradiction to disclose and consent for any source identity it would reveal; the edition it creates (DEC-80 item 2).
    Decides
    Disclose, acknowledge, and sign now or not.
    Can go wrong
    • NO_SIGNERS.
    • CASE_RATIFY_STALE (an acknowledgement or edit re-authored it).
    • ATTRIBUTION_UNCHOSEN / ATTRIBUTION_STATEMENT_STALE.
    • OPERATOR_TOKEN_CANNOT_RATIFY_CASE.
    • CASE_CONCLUSION_MOVED.
    • An undisclosed open record tension refuses the case; a disclosed one does not (DEC-84 (13)).
    • A load-bearing document whose co-attestation failed: retried, then acknowledged with a reason and marked 'self-attested only' (DEC-81 item 3).
    Hands to
    signer with case authority
    Trust risk
    A member who thinks they certify authenticity has been misled (Interaction Constructs section A).
    Open here
    • Decided, not built: the ceremony's requirements are owed (N345); the words of each step are the redesign's.

    Open ratification R2-R3; DEC-80; DEC-81; DEC-76 item 4; DEC-78 item 5

  15. signer with case authority · Minutes.
    Ratify each finding and its evidence
    Knows
    Pinned members awaiting signature.
    Decides
    Sign each.
    Can go wrong
    • RATIFY_FINDING_NOT_IN_A_RATIFIED_CASE.
    • RATIFY_NOT_EVIDENCE_OF_A_RATIFIED_CASE.
    • A finding ratified before its evidence does not link to it on the published graph (Publication section 3 rule 2 gap).
    Hands to
    anyone (public)
    Trust risk
    Irreversible: correction only by a new edition.

    Fixed ratification R4-R6; Publication section 3 rules 1-2

  16. anyone · Any time.
    Read and verify by hash
    Knows
    No account.
    Decides
    Trust or not.
    Can go wrong
    • Not published: stated.
    • A finding pinned by several cases: the reader chooses (FINDING_IN_SEVERAL_CASES).
    • Cases disagree on a finding's pair: CASES_DISAGREE, each case's pair shown.
    Hands to
    none
    Trust risk
    Reading strength as a single score, or authenticity into a hash.

    Fixed publication R8-R13, R26-R28

(c) Acting on a finding · 13 steps
  1. member · Minutes.
    Declare the standard (or adopt a proposal)
    Knows
    The standard's captured text; profile source match.
    Decides
    Which standard, from which capture.
    Can go wrong
    • MACHINE_CANNOT_DECLARE_STANDARD.
    • STANDARD_NO_CITE.
    • Source undetermined when the profile does not supply it.
    Hands to
    joined member
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed standards R1-R11

  2. joined member · Hours.
    Record a determination per standard
    Knows
    Published findings with frozen pairs; standards in force.
    Decides
    Compliant, noncompliant or unclear, per standard.
    Can go wrong
    • A standard not in force at the act's date: refused.
    • Any significance, rank or score key: refused (R8).
    • basis_changed later when a finding reopens.
    Hands to
    joined member
    Trust risk
    Judging significance through policy preference (Functional Architecture Function 4).

    Fixed conformance R1-R10; Operational Principle 1

  3. joined member · Hours.
    Record consequences
    Knows
    What the record can compute; what must be assessed.
    Decides
    Computed, assessed or undetermined; causation.
    Can go wrong
    • NOT_NONCOMPLIANT.
    • A part naming an individual: refused.
    • Totals only within one state.
    Hands to
    joined member
    Trust risk
    Composing harm into one headline figure (refused).

    Fixed consequences R1-R12

  4. joined member · Minutes.
    Open an escalation
    Knows
    The determination is live and noncompliant.
    Decides
    Whether to pursue, and why, in their own words (decided: DEC-89).
    Can go wrong
    • MACHINE_CANNOT_OPEN.
    • DETERMINATION_SUPERSEDED.
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed escalation R1; DEC-89

  5. joined member · Minutes.
    Or decline to escalate, saying why not now
    Knows
    The determination is live and noncompliant.
    Decides
    Why the group is not pursuing it now, in their own words.
    Can go wrong
    • Nothing may be recorded as significance, severity, priority, urgency or rank (conformance R8, escalation R19).
    Hands to
    the group; a later escalation, opened with its own reason, supersedes it and both stay readable
    Trust risk
    Until it is built, deciding not to escalate leaves no trace.
    Open here
    • Decided, not built: escalation, conformance and affordances requirements owed.

    Open DEC-89

  6. member · Minutes.
    Create a breach action to the office; set the risk tier
    Knows
    Counterparty by role and body from the profile.
    Decides
    Tier 1, 2 or 3 with a reason; governing laws.
    Can go wrong
    • Tier unset reads UNDETERMINED, never 1 (D-182).
    • MACHINE_CANNOT_SET_RISK_TIER.
    • LEASE_HELD (another member acting on the action).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    Legally unsophisticated filing creating adverse precedent (Tier 3).

    Fixed actions R1-R24; Design Requirement 8

  7. member (machine may prepare) · Hours to send; then the clock.
    Prepare, approve and record the filing sent
    Knows
    Draft with [UNFILLED] blanks; Tier 2 advisory first.
    Decides
    Approve text (edited or not); send outside the system; record sent.
    Can go wrong
    • FILING_STALE (tier, counterparty, determination or laws changed).
    • STILL_UNFILLED.
    • NOT_TIER3 / Tier 3 yields no template.
    Hands to
    the office (outside)
    Trust risk
    A machine-prepared filing mistaken for the group's approved words.

    Fixed filings R1-R7, R16-R18

  8. member · Statutory period (e.g. days).
    State the deadline with its legal basis
    Knows
    Profile deadlines, holidays.
    Decides
    The clock entry.
    Can go wrong
    • Every date carries its statute; overdue is derived, never silently stale (actions R35).
    Hands to
    plane (monitoring)
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed actions R32, R35; escalation R6

  9. plane · Runs on its own.
    The plane marks overdue and proposes the next stage
    Knows
    Clock dates; responses.
    Decides
    None: it proposes, never advances.
    Can go wrong
    • Not yet built: overdue marks and escalation prompts (monitoring R34-R35).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    The system appearing to decide the next stage.

    Fixed monitoring R34-R35, R44; escalation R2, R16-R17

  10. member · Days to weeks of waiting.
    Evaluate the response
    Knows
    Ledger entries; captured response.
    Decides
    complied / partial / denied / none, with reason.
    Can go wrong
    • Response arrives outside the system and must be captured.
    • Partial compliance does not stop the clock.
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    None named.

    Fixed escalation R10; Functional Architecture Function 5

  11. member · Minutes; proposals age visibly.
    Advance to legal tools or political accountability
    Knows
    Proposed edges with triggers.
    Decides
    Advance, decline with reason, suspend.
    Can go wrong
    • TRIGGER_NOT_MET naming what is missing.
    • ILLEGAL_STAGE.
    • NOT_OPEN.
    Hands to
    counsel or officials
    Trust risk
    Advocacy creeping in: stage 7 only enforces an existing requirement.

    Fixed escalation R8, R12-R16, R19

  12. member · Hours.
    Assemble a counsel packet (Tier 3)
    Knows
    Six sections from the record.
    Decides
    Name counsel.
    Can go wrong
    • MACHINE_CANNOT_NAME_COUNSEL.
    • basis_changed when a finding moves.
    Hands to
    named counsel
    Trust risk
    A packet read as legal advice or as fileable.

    Fixed filings R8-R13, R17

  13. member · Months.
    Record consequences addressed; end the escalation
    Knows
    Later compliant determination; addressed consequences.
    Decides
    End.
    Can go wrong
    • COMPLIANCE_NOT_RESTORED.
    • CONSEQUENCES_NOT_ADDRESSED.
    • CONSEQUENCES_UNDETERMINED.
    Hands to
    none

(d) The daily queue · 9 steps
  1. member · Minutes a day.
    Open the queue
    Knows
    Obligations, then queue findings, then conditions, grouped by case.
    Decides
    Where to start.
    Can go wrong
    • Truncated at the bound (said).
    • Home set UNDETERMINED: an ancestor out of view or past the depth bound (stated, never named).
    • NO_CLASS / NO_SUCH_KIND: the whole feed refused.
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    A truncated set indistinguishable from nobody caring (DEC-16).

  2. member · Seconds.

    Trust risk

  3. assignee · Minutes.

    Can go wrong

  7. joined member · Minutes; days if taken up.
    Resolve a tension in our own record, or dismiss a lead

    Decides

    Can go wrong

    • Explained without evidence: the mark softens and does not clear (DEC-77 item 1).
    Hands to

    Trust risk

    Open here
    • Decided, not built: requirements owed (N345).

  8. recipient · Minutes.

(f) From a knock to the record · 3 steps
  1. knocker (no account) · Seconds.
    Someone knocks with material
    Knows
    Nothing about the group.
    Decides
    What to send; an optional note and contact.
    Can go wrong
    • KNOCK_EMPTY, KNOCK_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE, KNOCK_ENVELOPE_TOO_LARGE.
    • RATE_IP / RATE_GLOBAL (published bound; no member-facing translation yet, D-508).
    Hands to
    member (doorbell inbox)
    Trust risk
    A 429 is a claim that needs a count behind it.
    Open here
    • What the knocker is told about what happens next, beyond the receipt and the optional knocker secret (DEC-78 item 3).

    Open capture R30-R32, R47-R56; Intake Doctrine section 2a

  2. member · Days.
    A member reads the inbox and resolves the knock
    Knows
    The material, its digest, the knocker's note (their own words, never evidence of truth) and contact (it stays in the inbox); a knocker pseudonym if the same secret was presented (DEC-78).
    Decides
    Pull (admits the material as a capture carrying the knock's receipt), discard, or leave new.
    Can go wrong
    • Two members resolving one knock at once is not specified.
    • An unknown knock: NO_SUCH_KNOCK, nothing written (capture R32, built T12).
    Hands to
    member
    Trust risk
    Unvetted material entering as if vouched: pulling commits the member to nothing more; vouching stays with release (DEC-78 item 1).
    Open here
    • Decided, not built: capture's requirements owed (N345).

    Open capture R32; DEC-78 items 1-4; Intake Doctrine §2a

  3. member · Months or years.
    What is known of the source grows
    Knows
    The source as it stood when the material was received, and each later disclosure, dated and attributed.
    Decides
    Record a disclosure; before publishing any identity detail, ask the source's consent.
    Can go wrong
    • A hostile exposure is recorded as the exposer's claim, never confirmed by the group without the source's consent (DEC-78 item 5(b)).
    • A stored name can be breached or subpoenaed: 'known to the group, not recorded' is a first-class choice (DEC-78 item 5(c)).
    Hands to
    project owner, at publishing (consent)
    Trust risk
    The group making a source more public than they chose (DEC-78 item 5(a)).
    Open here
    • Decided, not built: where the disclosure history lives is BOB's to place (N345).

    Open DEC-78 item 5

    Can go wrong

    • No surface sends draft= yet.
    Hands to
