Screens and their rules

The rules for each main screen: what it must show, what it must never show or do, and every state it must be designed for. Then the full list of screens the requirements imply, and whether the old interface has each one.

Queue (the attention layer)
  • Things that want me, grouped by the case they belong to; the one way work reaches a person. Interaction Constructs revision 0.2; DEC-10

Must show

  • Class of each item: obligation (do / forward / resolve), queue finding (adopt / defer / dismiss), condition (status or mute). Fixed queue R1, R12; NOTIFICATIONS.md
  • A queue finding must look derived: its source and derivation, and that nobody has judged it yet. Fixed queue R16; D-82
  • Agent-surfaced marker wherever a question appears. Fixed ai-runs R27; System Design row 12 (D-82)
  • Resolved by X on this date, for items someone else resolved. Fixed queue R39; DEC-16
  • What my mutes and snoozes are suppressing, with an undo. Fixed queue R14, R40; System Design row 12 (UI-97)
  • Each option's rung and set weight, pre-flighted for this caller. Fixed queue R12, R17; affordances R11
  • An obligation's own resolving act, as the item publishes it. Fixed queue R12; NOTIFICATIONS.md (REC-207)
  • The finding class labelled 'Noticed'. Fixed K356; queue R1 (built T12, N301)
  • A record contradiction as a duty that cannot be muted or dismissed, leaving only by resolution; a world one as a lead dismissed in one act with a fixed reason. Fixed DEC-76 item 3; DEC-84 (1)-(2), (17)
  • A newer capture that affects a member's reference, with adopt and keep. Fixed queue R9, R12 (built T12, N172)

Must never show or do

  • Never nag: one standing entry per (member, case); re-notify only on snooze expiry or something new. Fixed Interaction Constructs DEC-10; DEC-69
  • Never present a machine finding as an obligation; one check across 58 contracts is one proposal with 58 instances. Fixed Interaction Constructs section P (PROPOSAL); D-79
  • Never let a mute look like a record act, or a dismissal look personal. Fixed queue R14-R15, R20
  • Never mute an obligation. Fixed queue R19 (KIND_NOT_PERSONAL)
  • Never a severity ladder of warning / error colours in place of the three classes. Fixed NOTIFICATIONS.md 'three, not four severities'

States to design

  • Undetermined:
    • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
  • Withheld:
    • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
  • Refused:
    • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
  • Truncated:
    • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; queue R6; membership R48
  • Empty:
    • What does an empty queue say (nothing wants you, versus nothing could be read)? Open
  • Loading:
    • Not specified. Open
  • Stale:
    • A snoozed case's items are marked snoozed until the instant, not withheld by the plane. Fixed queue R40
  • Conflict:
    • Another member resolved a shared item: show attribution, not a gap. Fixed Interaction Constructs DEC-16
  • Ungrouped:
    • Items with no case sit ungrouped; a home set that is undetermined is stated. Fixed queue R7

Primary acts

  • resolve an obligation (taskresolve) affordances R1, R27
  • forward an obligation (taskforward) affordances R1, R27
  • defer / dismiss a queue finding (proposedispose) affordances R1-R2
  • mute / snooze queue R20-R21

Access and language

  • Phones supported, acceptably as a viewing MVP; features may be absent on some devices. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (devices)
  • Plain, reader-language meaning for every state, from one semantics table; every chip is a click-over disclosing its row. Fixed UI-KICKOFF state-semantics table
  • No accessibility standard (screen reader, keyboard, contrast) is named in canon; the UI plan records the hardening pass as absent. What level is required? Open
  • No language other than English is named in canon. Must the product or its published output be multilingual? Open

queue; Interaction Constructs; NOTIFICATIONS.md

ACT (the one motion for doing something to a record or a set)
  • Choose; see what it will refuse and why before it runs; author the reason; get a receipt. Interaction Constructs revision 0.2 (ACT)

Must show

  • Every act's button carries its rung's name and weight pips. Friction follows the rung: reversible inline; reasoned a reason field in place; terminal and attested a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs; irreversible only through the ceremony. Fixed affordances R2; DEC-87; Interaction Constructs §F
  • For a set: whether the act is all-or-nothing, report-and-proceed, or per-item, before acting. Fixed Interaction Constructs section S
  • The ballot's denominator: '2 of 3', never 'pending approval'; the divergence at two owners shown. Fixed Interaction Constructs section B
  • The receipt: what was written, by whom, when; a preview answers with nothing written. Fixed basis-versions R14
  • An act whose effect cannot be taken back outside the record opens the full dialog stating that effect, whatever its rung: attributing a name, sharing a lead, naming a person in the registry, setting the group bar. Fixed DEC-88 item 4
  • A recommendation, labelled machine work, is shown first with its reason and accepted in one attributed act; facts the record holds are filled in with their source. Fixed DEC-77 item 3

Must never show or do

  • Never compute a refusal; only render one received from the plane (publication or dry run). Fixed Interaction Constructs RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-8)
  • Never prefill, suggest, template or complete a justification; assembling the member's own prior words is allowed. Fixed Interaction Constructs section J (boundary of the prefill rule)
  • Never land an act on rows the member did not see; nothing preselected; visibility may only shrink a selection. Fixed Interaction Constructs section S (D-35, DEC-69)
  • Never offer an act the caller's capability lacks (absent, not greyed). Fixed Membership v2 section 5
  • Never one dialog for every act with the rung merely highlighted. Fixed DEC-87; Interaction Constructs revision 0.2
  • Never enter a value the member never saw under their name; nothing silently preselected. Fixed DEC-77 item 3(c)

States to design

  • Undetermined:
    • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
  • Withheld:
    • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
  • Refused:
    • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
  • Truncated:
    • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; queue R6; membership R48
  • Empty:
    • Not specified. Open
  • Loading:
    • Not specified. Open
  • Stale:
    • A selection whose members moved: report says what moved; refuse hands over nothing (SET_MOVED). Fixed retrieval R19-R20
  • Conflict:
    • A second member changed the object first: CAS_STALE naming expected and got; never merged. An action's 30-second lease held by another: LEASE_HELD. Fixed promotion R1; actions R16
  • Expired:
    • A selection handle unknown, released or expired: NO_SUCH_SELECTION, one answer. Fixed retrieval R19
  • No rung yet:
    • An act whose rung is not yet ruled is treated as reasoned. Fixed DEC-87

Question workspace (the inquiry page)
  • Where a member builds a question's support, compares accounts and concludes. Case Making; basis-versions; inquiry

Must show

  • Legs with role (supports / cuts against), earned grade, what it was capped from and why; ungraded legs named as not load-bearing. Fixed inquiry R13-R14, R32; strength R3
  • The strength pair per axis (capture, connection) with testimony beside; unrated and undetermined stated; what-ifs labelled as exploration. Fixed strength R1-R10, R18-R20
  • Versions, their states, which is CURRENT per project, and each project's conclusion history. Fixed basis-versions R5, R11, R22
  • Structure authored before strength is shown; restructuring after seeing is recorded and attributed. Fixed DECISIONS.md DEC-32 enactment note (UI-27)
  • A per-leg control asking whether a newer capture holds the passage. Fixed System Design row 4 (UI-96); content R29
  • Who reopened, divided or set aside the question, when and why. Fixed inquiry R19, R24
  • Machine-suggested versions labelled as machine work, with the run that made them. Fixed AI Roles rule 3; run-productions R5
  • Each leg's grade on the one A-D scale with its scale's icon; hover names the weakest document or link and how to raise it; a click highlights that leg. Fixed DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M
  • A tension mark wherever two conflicting points sit together (supporting and cutting-against legs, alternative versions, two projects' conclusions), opening the 'how do these differ?' clarifier. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-84 (3)

Must never show or do

  • Never a single confidence score or a case-level strength. Fixed AI Roles rule 9; strength R18
  • Never draft framing or a conclusion for the member. Fixed Case Making section 4a
  • Never let one project's act move another project's stance. Fixed basis-versions R31; inquiry R39

Fixed basis-versions R31; DEC-84 (3)

    • A tension mark opens the clarifier: different time or occasion, part or scope, meaning, observer or method, not the same subject; one of them is wrong; they really conflict; not sure. Only the last two open the contradiction inquiry. Explained without evidence, the mark softens and does not clear. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-84 (16)

inquiry; basis-versions; strength; citation

Tension mark, clarifier and contradiction inquiry
  • Meet a conflict where its two points already sit, settle it there in one act when possible, and escalate to the dedicated contradiction inquiry only when it will not dissolve. DEC-77 item 1; DEC-76 item 2

Must show

  • Both sides in the record's own words, each with source, date, document type and why the two were paired (the compare view). Fixed DEC-77 item 2
  • The clarifier's choices: different time or occasion, part or scope, meaning, observer or method, not the same subject (multi-select); one of them is wrong; they really conflict; not sure. Fixed DEC-77 item 1
  • Facts the record holds filled in with their source; a recommendation of which respects may differ, labelled machine work, shown first with its reason. Fixed DEC-77 item 3; DEC-84 (5)
  • In the contradiction inquiry: the assumptions checklist as its working view, the timeline for a reversal, the competing-hypotheses matrix for a conflict that will not dissolve or a conflict of norms. Fixed DEC-77 item 2; K447 point 9
  • The mark's state wherever a side appears: in tension, explained not yet shown (softened), stale with its reason, qualified. Fixed DEC-77 items 1-2; DEC-84 (7)

Must never show or do

  • Never say which side is wrong, or that a conflict is genuine, on the machine's word. Fixed DEC-84 (5); DEC-24
  • Never preselect a choice; never fill the member's reason from the machine's. Fixed DEC-77 item 3(c); DEC-84 (14)
  • Never let a record duty be muted, dismissed or set aside; never mark aspirations as in contradiction (they are in contact). Fixed DEC-84 (2); DEC-77 item 1
  • Never show a precision or unrelated label as a tension, and never reveal a side the viewer may not see. Fixed CONTRADICTION-PRESENT-RESOLVE-DESIGN.md §4; contradiction R10

States to design

  • Undetermined:
    • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
  • Withheld:
    • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
  • Refused:
    • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
  • Softened:
    • Explained without evidence on the screen: the mark softens and does not clear; a record duty stays in the queue. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-76 item 1
  • Stale:
    • A side marked wrong: stale with its reason, never deleted; holders get a re-evaluation notice. Fixed DEC-84 (7), (16)
  • Empty:
    • Not specified. Open
  • Loading:
    • Not specified. Open

  • one of them is wrong DEC-84 (16)

  • accept a recommendation DEC-77 item 3(b)
  • dismiss a lead (one fixed reason) DEC-84 (17)

case-authoring; Publication

Publication ceremony
  • The heaviest act and the only way to it: five steps (what becomes permanent; what this rests on; what you are leaving out; the edition this creates; sign), after a pre-flight that runs the real refusals first. It replaces the placeholder. DEC-80; Publication §5

Must show

  • What is about to become permanent, and what it will and will not claim (bytes, address, date, route; not authenticity). Fixed Interaction Constructs section A
  • An explicit key act; signer and deliverer as two facts. Fixed Interaction Constructs revision 0.2; ratification R12
  • Correction moves forward: a new edition, never erasure. Fixed affordances R2 (IRREVERSIBLE_CORRECTION_PATH); DEC-19
  • Before the first screen, the pre-flight's real refusals, writing nothing: below the bar, an uncleared hunch, no signers. Fixed DEC-80 item 3
  • Step three: exclusions and their acknowledgements, what was searched, the bias acknowledged, any unresolved record contradiction for the owner to disclose, and consent for any source identity the case would reveal. Fixed DEC-80 item 2; DEC-76 item 4; DEC-78 item 5
  • Each document's capture grade and whether it is co-attested; a missing co-attestation retried first, then acknowledged with a reason and marked 'self-attested only'. Fixed DEC-81 items 1, 3
  • Signing in the browser with the owner's registered browser-held key, confirmed by passphrase or device unlock. Fixed DEC-80 item 4

Must never show or do

  • Never a button in a row of buttons; the ceremony is the safeguard. Fixed Interaction Constructs revision 0.2 'Where FEWER costs something'
  • Never let a machine credential or bearer token deliver an attested act. Fixed AI Roles rule 4 (D-421)
  • Never discover NO_SIGNERS only after signing. Fixed Interaction Constructs RULED 2026-08-01
  • Never refuse a case because a contradiction exists; refuse only one left undisclosed. Fixed DEC-76 item 4; DEC-84 (13)
  • Never refuse outright a document that cannot be co-archived. Fixed DEC-81 item 3(d)

  • Refused:

  • Truncated:

  • Empty:
    • No placeholder: the ceremony replaces it. Fixed DEC-80 item 1

Standards, determinations, consequences, actions and escalation
  • Where the group measures a government act against standards and pursues it to compliance restored and consequences addressed. layer 9 modules; Design Requirement 7; Operational Principle 6

Must show

  • basis_changed flags naming causes. Fixed conformance R10; consequences R8; filings R12

  • For an obligation-against-act finding: Criteria, Condition, Cause (only when evidenced, else 'cause not established'), Effect, Recommendation (a proposed action). Fixed DEC-77 item 2; DEC-84 (10)

  • Never name an individual except in official role. Fixed consequences R10; Design Requirement 6

Assistant (persistent tag, dialog, running-session surface)
  • Free natural-language input on every surface; FIND needs no ceremony, CREATE needs confirmation, ACT runs the act's four beats. Interaction Constructs section P (DEC-27)

  • What it understood, before anything is created. Fixed Interaction Constructs section P

  • A running indicator to teammates, not the reasoning; one running-session surface for all AI features. Fixed AI Roles rule 6, section 4
  • That the capability is unavailable, when no credential resolves; never silently degraded. Fixed AI Roles section 6
  • A voice transcription for correction before it becomes anything. Fixed Interaction Constructs section P (Voice)
  • A panel docked beside the surface it serves: at the side on wide screens, at the bottom on narrow ones; the member may switch edges, and the choice is remembered. Fixed DEC-90
  • Expandable by a draggable divider and an 'expand' control, always leaving some of the surface visible; it shrinks back by itself when a wizard step needs the member to act; one click restores the default. Fixed DEC-90
  • A wizard step highlights the real control; the member performs every act on the real surface. Fixed DEC-90

How heavy each act is

Every act carries a weight, its rung, and the rung decides how much friction the act gets (Bob, 2026-09-29, DEC-87). Friction is kept as low as possible so the tool fades and the work stays in focus, yet a heavier act slows the member down, even for a moment, before it is taken.

  • Can be undone happens inline.
  • Needs a stated reason opens a reason field in place.
  • Ends something and Signed, on the record open a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs.
  • Cannot be undone only through the publication ceremony (DEC-80).

Every act's button shows its rung's name and weight mark (DEC-82). Weights come from the affordances module. Bob gave the 57 acts that had none their rungs on 2026-09-29 (DEC-88: 26 reversible, 29 reasoned, 2 terminal), and they are shown here at those rungs; the module takes them up when BOB moves them, and each says so. 18 acts here still show Weight not yet set: some because no operation of their own has been located, others because they are decided but not yet built and their rung is not yet assigned; each is treated as needing a stated reason until BOB assigns it.

Six acts are judgement calls that carry high friction whatever their rung, because friction follows the consequence in the world, and a heavy consequence is not a new rung (DEC-88):

  • Choose attribution for a case edition (Firsthand observation, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a name that becomes permanent at publication.
  • Share to a project (Lead, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a disclosure that cannot be un-read.
  • Register a subject (Subject, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a person named in the registry.
  • Set the group default bar (Strength, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a gate on the whole group's work.
  • Have the assistant work the objective (Objective, progress and gaps, Needs a stated reason): High friction, the lightest of the six (DEC-88): its reason field opens in place with the run's budget and scope shown beside it.
  • Approve (Filing draft and counsel packet, Ends something): terminal, so the full dialog stating what it ends is already its own.

An act that is decided but not yet built, such as “Decline to escalate” (DEC-89), is listed at its rung and marked as not yet built.

Cannot be undone 1 acts · Only through the publication ceremony (DEC-80).

Words

The product's own terms, in plain words, with the internal names behind them and the words they are easily confused with. Each state in Part 3 shows its member-facing word first. 108 of those are working names proposed from the definitions, marked working name, for the design to settle.

TermPlain meaningInternal namesEasily confused with
GroupThe people who run one copy of CivicOS together; it can be one person. instance, group slug, group-instance
Copy (of CivicOS)The group's own installation, in the group's own hosting account. instance, bio-plane, Workera copy of a document (a capture)
FounderThe person who claimed the copy first; the root of trust. rootOfTrust, claim, bootstrap credential
MemberA person enrolled in the group with a handle and password. member:<id>, handle, cover
AdministratorA member who manages membership and group settings, but has no power inside a project they have not joined. administer, adminendorse, adminremove
CapabilityA permission a member holds, such as contribute; without it the act is not shown. capabilities, contribute, create_projects, membercaps
InvitationA one-time link that lets a new person enrol; once used it is gone. invite, invitelook, enroll
ProjectA shared workspace where some members build a case together; hidden unless made discoverable. project, projectinvite, projectjoin, visibilitya question: a project holds questions, it is not one
OwnerA project member who can invite, remove and publish for that project. project_owner, projectowneradd, ownerMath
Joined participantA member who has joined a project and may act inside it. joined, project_participant, roster
DocumentAnything the group holds as evidence: a captured page or file, or a member's own written observation. information, bundle, information bundle
CaptureFetching a public document and keeping its exact bytes, with where and when they came from. acquire, capture, captureShacapture grade (how well the capture is established)
Release (vouch for)A named member states a document appears to be what it claims; not that it is true. release, verified, promotion R31publish: releasing keeps it inside the group
RetireTake a document out of use; it is kept forever but can no longer be cited. retire, retired
ProvenanceThe record of where a document came from and how it reached the group. provenance, route, provenancechain
Grade (A-D)A letter for how easily someone else could check something for themselves, never whether it is true. Capture, connection, testimony and subject match share one A-D meaning, each with its own colour and icon, and are never added up. DEC-82; System Design §4capture grade, connection grade, testimony gradestrength, which is the weakest grade along a chain
PassageA marked part of a document (a page region or text span) that can be cited on its own. content, contentmint, mint
Reading (of a document)What the system extracted from a document's bytes, with how reliable that extraction is. extraction, tier, pdfstructurereading of a question's evidence (a basis version), which the old UI also calls a reading
TranscriptionA member typing what part of a document says; it counts more once a different member confirms it. transcribe, transcriptionattest
SubjectA person, office, place, law or thing the record knows by name and aliases. entity, entitycreate, resolve
ConnectionA link between documents through a shared subject, found by the machine or chosen by a member. connection, connectionchoose, connect
ThemeA member's own idea with a test that material passes or fails; it gathers material but never proves anything. theme, themedeclare, themeplace
Flow (progression)How something is supposed to go, as ordered stages, so a missing or late stage shows up. progression, progressiondefine, thread, discharge
LeadA member's tip or suspicion and where to look; never evidence itself. lead, leadshare, leadlook
Firsthand observationSomething a member saw or heard, recorded in their own words as a document. testify, firsthand-observationobservation (a look: whether anyone looked and what they found)
LookA record of whether anyone looked for something and what they found: 'Nobody looked', looked and absent, could not tell, present. 'Nobody looked' has its own look and fixed sentence, distinct from Undetermined ('could not tell', DEC-86). observation-log R1-R3; DEC-86observation, NEVER_LOOKED, LOOKED_ABSENT, LOOKED_INDETERMINATE, PRESENT, frontierfirsthand observation
QuestionSomething the group is trying to answer, and the evidence it rests on. inquiry, open, surfaced, focus, problem
FindingA question the group has answered, with what would prove the answer wrong. Case Making §Naming; K356inquiry (concluded), conclude, falsifier'Noticed': the queue class for something the system noticed that nobody has judged (K356)
LegOne piece of evidence cited for or against a question. leg, cite, citation, basis
Cite / severAdd a piece of evidence to a question, or withdraw it with a reason; withdrawn evidence stays listed. cite, sever, reinstate
Reading of the evidence (basis version)One way of arranging a question's evidence; members accept or turn it down, and a project picks one to stand on. basis-version, versionaccept, versioncurrent, CURRENTreading of a document (extraction)
StrengthHow well a question is supported: the weakest capture and weakest connection, shown as a pair, never one score. strength R1-R10; DEC-44; DEC-82strength pair, inquirystrength, graded / unrated / undeterminedgrade: strength is read off the weakest grade
BarThe strength a project (or the group by default) requires before it will publish. strength R14-R16; DEC-82required_strength, strengthbar
UndeterminedWhat we do not know, and why: one mark with a line that always says 'because…', shown the same way everywhere, never as an error or a zero (DEC-86). DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §Uundetermined, UNDETERMINEDits four neighbours, each with its own look and its own fixed sentence, never shared (DEC-86): withheld (it exists, you may not see it), unrated (nothing graded yet), nobody looked, refused (the system declined an act)
Lens (declared bias)The group's or project's stated viewpoint, adopted openly so readers can weigh it. bias, bias-set, biasadopt, manifest
Bias debt / hunch debtBias debt: the lens changed since work was done, disclosed. Hunch debt: a connection graded ahead of its evidence, must be cleared before publishing. biasdebt, biasdebtresolve, huncheach other: bias debt never blocks, hunch debt does
CaseWhat a project publishes: a set of findings and evidence, signed, that anyone can check. case, publish, case document, editiona question or finding (before DEC-72 a case was a phase of a question)
Review copyAn unsigned draft of a case shown to chosen reviewers inside the group; never leaves. casedraft, reviewgrant, reviewcopy, reviewcommentpublication
Statement and acknowledgementThe case's statement of what it leaves out; others confirm they read it. exclusion statement, statementack, completeness
Sign / ratifyAn owner signs with a registered key: the case document, then each finding and its evidence. Decided: signing happens in the browser with a browser-held key, producing the same signature strangers check. ratification R2-R6; DEC-80caseratify, ratify, signing key, SSHSIGrelease (vouching needs no key)
PublishPut a case across the fence for anyone to read, through the publication ceremony; it cannot be taken back, only corrected by a new edition. Publication §5; DEC-80publish, irreversible, edition
VerifyAnyone checks a published case against its hash without the group's help. verify, manifest_sha
StandardA rule, law or commitment the group holds in its own words, with its source and period. standard, standarddeclare, standardadopt
DeterminationA member's judgement of whether a government act complied with named standards: complies, does not, or unclear. determination, determine, conformancefinding: a determination rests on findings
ConsequenceThe harm or cost of a breach, computed from the record, assessed by a member, or undetermined. consequence, addressedrecord
ActionSomething the group asks of someone outside it, with the reply, the clock and the correspondence. action, actionmove, actioncorrespond, clockan act (any button that changes the record)
Filing / counsel packetA prepared request or complaint text, and a bundle of facts assembled for a lawyer; not legal advice. filing, filingPrepare, counselPacket
EscalationSeven stages of pressing a breach, from documenting it to political accountability. Opening one needs a written reason; a member may instead record why the group declines to escalate (DEC-89, not yet built). escalation R1-R21; DEC-89escalation, escalationOpen, stage 1-7
ClockA deadline on an action, always with the law or promise it comes from. clock, clock_next, overdue, clockPropose
QueueEverything that wants a member, grouped by case: to-dos, things noticed, and signals. queue R1, R6; K356queue, OBLIGATION, FINDING, CONDITION, task
Mute / snooze vs disposeMuting or snoozing quiets your own queue only; deferring or dismissing is a recorded decision for everyone. queuemute, queuesnooze, proposedispose, dispose
ActAnything a member does that changes the record: choose it, see what it would refuse, give the reason, get a receipt. ACT, affordances, opaction (an outward request)
RungHow heavy an act is: reversible, reasoned, terminal, attested, irreversible. Friction follows it: reversible inline, reasoned a reason field in place, terminal and attested a full dialog, irreversible only through the ceremony; every button shows its rung's name and weight pips (DEC-87). affordances R2; K356; DEC-87RUNGS, RUNG_LADDER, rung_absencethe path to publication (release, stand behind, ground, conclude, accept, ratify, publish), a sequence of acts (K356)
Set weightHow an act behaves on many items at once: all or nothing, report what moved, or item by item. weight, refuse, report, per-itemrung (weight was the ladder's old name)
Assistant (AI run)A bounded machine session that looks, suggests and asks for captures; it never decides. It appears as a panel docked beside the screen it serves, expandable, never floating (DEC-90). ai-runs Purpose; DEC-90ai-run, airunopen, run, investigate, extract
Machine-work labelThe one mark on anything a machine proposed or recommended, saying who asked for it; it is not evidence until a member acts on it. AI Roles §3 rule 3; DEC-77; DEC-90proposal, suggested, actor_class: machine
Capture requestA request for the system to fetch a public address later, which the source may refuse. capturerequest, capturerequestretry
KnockMaterial handed over through the doorbell by someone without an account, waiting for a member to decide. Its source is an unnamed knocker, never 'unknown'. capture R30-R32; DEC-78knock, inbox, inboxresolve
Objective / goal / aspirationObjective: what a project needs, with a test. Goal: something being pursued. Aspiration: a standing value. objective, objectivecondition, goal, aspiration, intent
Re-evaluationA notice that something your work rests on changed, asking for a second look. reevaluation, reevaluationrecord, versionadopt, versionkeep
MonitoringRe-checking a document's public address on a schedule to see if it changed or vanished. monitor, monitoring, cadence
Jurisdiction profileLocal rules and deadlines for a place, chosen by an administrator. profiles, jurisdictions, profilesSet
Keys and credentialsA signing key proves who signed; an assistant key lets a machine act; a source login opens a paywalled source. signeradd, aicredentialmint, capturecredentialsupply
Path to publicationThe sequence of acts a finding passes on its way out: release, stand behind, ground, conclude, accept, ratify, publish. K356; System Design §3 row 12rung ladder (old canon name)rung (how heavy one act is)
NoticedA queue item the system noticed that nobody has judged yet, such as a missing step in a flow. K356; queue R1; N301 (built T12)FINDING (queue class), class_labelsfinding (a concluded question); the condition disposition also called 'noticed' (open question 28)
WithheldSomething exists that is not yours to see; said only as 'this is incomplete', never what or how many. DEC-36; DEC-86; strength R6out_of_view, DEC-36Undetermined
UnratedNothing is graded yet: the strength rests on nothing so far. strength R3; DEC-86unratedUndetermined (could not tell)
RefusedThe system declined an act and says why, then what to do next or instead. DEC-83; DEC-86; membership R84reason, translation, remedy, NOT_AN_ADMINan error
Measures mapThe one page that places every scale: letters grade evidence, bars show progress, weights mark acts. DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §Mmeasures-map.html
Stage (of a project)Where a project stands: Forming, Investigating, Matured, or Closed with its reason. Worked out from the record, never set by hand, except closing. State Rules §4.3; publication R44-R49; K362; DEC-79projectStage, op=projectstage, stagesescalation stage (1-7)
Readiness (of a case)How far a project's case has come: Draft, Internally checked, Externally compliant, Distributed; each shown by its own evidence. State Rules §4.3; K364; publication R46readiness, rungs, work productrung (weight of an act)
Publication ceremonyThe five steps an owner walks through to publish: what becomes permanent, what it rests on, what it leaves out, the edition it creates, and signing. Publication §5; DEC-80UI-17, REC-15 (pre-flight), publish, caseratifyrelease (vouching, inside the group)
Pre-flightThe check that runs before the ceremony's first screen and says what would stop publication, writing nothing. DEC-80 item 3REC-15, UNCLEARED_HUNCH, BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH
Co-attested / self-attested onlyCo-attested: an outside timestamp and an outside archive copy back our copy of a document. Self-attested only: that outside backing failed, and the case says so. DEC-81; Intake Doctrine §3; provenance R32-R33co-attestation, RFC 3161 timestamp, co-archivevouching (release)
Pull (a knock)A member brings doorbell material into the record as a captured document; it commits them to nothing more. DEC-78 item 1inboxresolve, pulledrelease (vouching for it)
Source (of material)Who a piece of material came from, even unnamed; what is known of who they are may grow, step by step, each step dated. DEC-78 items 2, 5source, disclosure historycapturing member (who brought it in, never its source)
Knocker secretAn optional secret a knocker gives with each knock so the group can tell the knocks come from one person, without learning who they are. DEC-78 item 3knocker secret, pseudonym, keyed digesta name or identity
ContradictionTwo statements that cannot both hold in the same respect. Most apparent conflicts dissolve once the respect that differs is found. DEC-76; DEC-84 (3)contradiction candidate, world, record, DISSOLVED, CORRECTED, GENUINEa difference of opinion between two projects (plurality, each attributed)
Tension markThe mark on anything caught in an unresolved conflict in our own record; it opens a short 'how do these differ?' question. DEC-77 items 1-2tensionsOn, in_tension, softened, stale, qualifiedstale (a cited passage re-read)
Accepting a recommendationTaking the machine's suggestion in one act; the record keeps that you accepted it, and which one. DEC-77 item 3(b); DEC-84 (14)acceptance basis, accepted, unaided, chose_otherwisethe machine deciding (it never does)
Decline to escalateRecording, in your own words, why the group is not pursuing a breach now; a later escalation can supersede it. DEC-89(no op yet)declining a proposed escalation stage (escalationdecline)
Origin markWho made this: Ours, Another group's, Accepted by our group, Not yet evaluated, or Flagged. DEC-92; Roadmap §11(no module yet)a trust score (there is none)
Assistant panelWhere the assistant talks: docked beside the screen it serves, expandable, never floating over the work. DEC-90ASSISTANT-PILOT, wizard

Display primitives

The few ways the product shows certainty, absence, weight and restriction. Each should look the same wherever it appears.

The measures map: letters grade evidence, bars show progress, weights mark acts

one frame for every scale a member meets. One visual form per scale, used everywhere and for nothing else; colour marks the scale, never the value; every mark opens to its plain question; an answer is only as strong as the weakest thing it depends on; a grade tells how easily someone else could check it, never whether it is true

Where: every grade, bar and act button

DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M; docs/development/ux-substrate/measures-map.html (approved)

Undetermined (one component, always 'because…')

what we do not know and why: one component with a fixed mark, identical everywhere, and a mandatory 'because…' line; first-class, never an error, never a zero, written as a named term. A newcomer meets it first, beside strength against the bar

Where: legs, strength axes, capture route, risk tier, standard source and in-force, consequences, home sets, deliverer, profiles, a stage the record cannot read

DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U, §M; System Design §1

Its four neighbours: Withheld, Unrated, Nobody looked, Refused

each visibly different from Undetermined and from each other, each with one fixed sentence pattern of its own, never shared: Withheld (exists, not yours to see; incompleteness only, never what or how many); Unrated (nothing graded yet: rests on nothing); Nobody looked (no one searched there; not the same as looked and absent); Refused (the system declined an act, naming its reason). None is dressed as an error

Where: wherever Undetermined can appear

DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U; DEC-36; strength R3; observation-log R1

Evidence letters A-D (one shared scale)

every evidence scale (capture, connection, testimony, subject match) shares one A-D meaning: A is easiest for someone else to check, D rests on a person's word. Each scale has its own colour and descriptive icon (document, link, speech, person) and the same badge shape; scales are never added up and never take disjoint letters

Where: captures, subject matches, connections, legs, flow instances

DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M; System Design §4; entities R9; provenance R24-R27

Strength pair and the bar

the weakest capture and weakest connection, side by side, testimony beside; graded, 'Unrated' or 'Undetermined' with its reason; never one score. In the project workspace each question shows its two badges and one phrase against the bar ('Meets the bar', 'Short on connection', 'Short on capture', 'Unrated'); hover names the weakest document or link and how to raise it; 'Unrated' has its own treatment and fixed sentence, distinct from Undetermined (DEC-86)

Where: questions, versions, the project workspace, published cases, determinations

strength R1-R10; publication R11; DEC-44; DEC-82 (question 5); DEC-86

Progress bars (stage, readiness, source identity)

each ladder has its own colour family and texture (solid, striped, dotted); dashed means 'not yet evaluated'; every step is named, reached or not. A project's stage stacks one bar per stage reached; hover, focus or tap discloses more and a click on a reached step shows how it was reached; 'closed' shows its reason and never reads as a finished stack. Readiness shows each rung by its own evidence; a published case reads Distributed, never Draft

Where: project home, the project's cases, a source's identity

DEC-79; DEC-82; K364; DEC-78 item 5; State Rules §4.3; publication R44-R49

Weight marks on acts (rung and friction)

every act's weight: reversible < reasoned < terminal < attested < irreversible. every act's button carries its rung's name and weight pips, so weight is read before the click. Friction follows the rung: reversible inline; reasoned a reason field in place; terminal and attested a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs; irreversible only through the ceremony. An act whose effect cannot be taken back outside the record opens the full dialog whatever its rung. An act with no rung is treated as reasoned

Where: every act option

affordances R2-R3, R27; Interaction Constructs §revised set, §F; DEC-87; DEC-88

Set weight

refuse (all or nothing), report (proceed, say what moved), per-item (each applied or kept with reason), single

Where: bulk acts on selections and queue sets

affordances R1; record-core R50-R52

Look states: 'Nobody looked', looked and absent, could not tell, partial, present

which absence is true, at which of four levels; 'Nobody looked' is written as a named term, as Undetermined is; 'nobody looked' has its own treatment and fixed sentence, never Undetermined's (DEC-86)

Where: frontier, leads, run logs, searched section of a case

observation-log R1-R3; skills R19; case-authoring R17; DEC-82; DEC-86

Content-axis states

indexed full / partial / none, not extracted, undetermined

Where: documents and passages

observation-log R1, R12

'Machine work' label

the one treatment shared by every machine output: anything a machine proposed or recommended, never evidence until a member acts; with attribution ('the assistant did this, at <member>'s request'). A recommendation is labelled, shown first with its reason, and accepted in one attributed act the record remembers

Where: suggested versions, proposed readings, standards, comparisons, laws, tiers, filings, contradiction labels and recommendations, the assistant panel

AI Roles §3 rule 3; DEC-77 item 3; DEC-90; filings R5; standards R9

Origin mark (partly ruled)

who made this, in the same family as 'Machine work': Ours; Another group's (a published edition, signature verified); Accepted by our group (a reasoned act, with its reason); Not yet evaluated; Flagged (only a member's recorded evaluation naming specific issues, never a machine's). Ambient; hover shows the fact behind it; never composed with grades or strength into a trust score. 'Meets standards' waits for an evaluator of incoming work; more is Bob's to decide later

Where: information from other groups, cited work, documents

DEC-92; Roadmap §11 (revised); inquiry R7

Tension mark

on any side of an unresolved record contradiction: in tension, softened (explained, not yet shown), stale (corrected, with its reason) or qualified (dissolved, with its qualifier); a world conflict shows as a quiet lead. Opens the 'how do these differ?' clarifier; published as an attributed sentence

Where: legs, versions, determinations, cases, subjects, changed captures, two projects' conclusions

DEC-76; DEC-77 items 1-2; DEC-84 (3), (7); CONTRADICTION-PRESENT-RESOLVE-DESIGN.md §5

Co-attested / 'self-attested only'

a published case states each document's capture grade and whether it is co-attested (a trusted timestamp and a third-party co-archive); a document whose co-attestation failed is marked 'self-attested only', with the owner's acknowledgement

Where: documents, the ceremony, published cases

DEC-81 items 1, 3; Intake Doctrine §3

Agent-surfaced marker

this question was raised by the assistant

Where: wherever a question is listed or shown

System Design §3 row 12 (D-82); ai-runs R27

Withheld / out of view

something exists you may not see: stated as incompleteness, never identity or count; its own treatment and one fixed sentence, never Undetermined's (DEC-86)

Where: queue homes, strength members, re-evaluation dependents, observation rows

queue R7 (DEC-36); strength R6 (withheld whole, built T12, K360); reevaluation R3; observation-log R13; DEC-86

Existence-only project

you see the name of a discoverable project, nothing inside

Where: project directory, acts naming a project

membership R44, R48, R77

Stale / newer version notice

a passage was cited under an earlier reading or a newer capture exists (affected / undetermined); a notice, never a silent change

Where: legs, cites, claims, the queue

content R22; reevaluation R14; queue R9, R12 (built T12, N172)

basis_changed flag

something a determination, consequence or packet rests on moved; nothing recomputed

Refused: the standard sentence, then what to do

every refusal names its reason in plain words before anything runs; where refusals of one condition share a code, the member reads the shared sentence first and then the act's own next step or alternative, as part of what they read; 'Refused' has its own treatment and fixed sentence, never Undetermined's (DEC-86)

Where: every act

Contradiction candidateBuilt, with gapsTwo statements that seem to conflict. A contradiction is conditional: two statements conflict only if they cannot both hold in the same respect (subject, time or occasion, scope, meaning, observer or method). The machine pairs and labels candidates; a member resolves them.

Module contradiction contradiction R1-R23 (IDENTIFY: pairing and machine labels, built; nothing shows a candidate to a member); DEC-76, DEC-77, DEC-84 (PRESENT and RESOLVE, decided); docs/development/CONTRADICTION-PRESENT-RESOLVE-DESIGN.md (reviewed K447); N345

What a member sees: key (K1-K4), both sides, label: world / record / precision / unrelated / undetermined, run, the respects the machine recommends may differ, labelled machine work; never which side is wrong nor a genuine kind (DEC-84 (5)), whether the member chose unaided or accepted a recommendation, and which one (DEC-77 item 3)

Shown as: a tension mark inline, wherever the two points already sit together, opening the 'how do these differ?' clarifier; the dedicated screen only as the escalation (DEC-77 item 1), six forms, one per purpose: compare view, timeline, assumptions checklist, competing-hypotheses matrix, Criteria-Condition-Cause-Effect-Recommendation for an obligation-against-act finding, in-place tension marker (DEC-77 item 2), published: an unresolved record contradiction disclosed on the case as an attributed sentence; publication discloses, never blocks (DEC-76 item 4; DEC-84 (13)), a stale side moves nothing by itself: whoever relies on it gets a re-evaluation notice, and the mark shows wherever the side does (DEC-84 (7)), the acceptance rate of recommendations, per kind with counts, reviewed at 95% or more over 30 or more offered (provisional; K447 point 15)

Lifecycle

  1. Machine suggestion, not judged working nameproposedmachine judgement stored; nobody has judged it
  2. Leadlead (world, or undetermined)a conflict about the world: taken up or dismissed with a reason; never a duty
  3. In tensionin tension (record)a conflict in our own record: a duty for the joined members of every project drawing on either side, leaving only by resolution; each side marked wherever shown
  4. Explained, not yet shownexplained, not yet showna member said how the two differ without evidence on the screen: the mark softens and does not clear
  5. Being looked into working nametaken upopened as a contradiction inquiry, the dedicated screen
  6. Resolved: dissolved / corrected / genuine working nameresolvedits kind recorded: dissolved (a respect differed; both stay, each qualified), corrected (the wrong side goes stale with its reason, never deleted) or genuine (double-speak promoted to a finding; obligation against act routed into a determination; conflict of norms; held irreconcilable)
  7. Dismissed working namedismisseda lead set down in one act with one fixed reason (same fact at different precision; not about the same matter; a real conflict, not pursued now) and optional words

Queue item (and task)Built, with gapsOne thing that wants this member's attention, grouped under every case it belongs to: an obligation a person must do, a finding nobody has judged, or a condition about the copy's own machinery.

Module queue, affordances queue Purpose, Terms, R1-R29, R39-R40; Interaction Constructs §QUEUE, DEC-10, DEC-16, DEC-36; K356; N172; N330; DEC-76

What a member sees: class and kind, home cases (ancestors to depth 6), summary, detail, basis, age, assignee, options (acts with rung and weight), disposition, item kinds added in T12: a newer capture affects a reference (adopt or keep), an objective gap (N172); specified T14: source modified or removed, archive fallback eligible, recheck due (N330), a record contradiction waits as a duty until resolved, never muted or dismissed; a world one as a lead (decided: DEC-76 item 3; DEC-84 (1)-(2))

Shown as: out_of_view / undetermined home set (R7), 'resolved by X on date' (R39; built T12 except for bias debts, specified T14, N326), snoozed marker (R40, built T12), FINDING never reads as an obligation (R16)

Lifecycle

  1. To do: open → passed on → doneOBLIGATION task: open → forwarded → resolvedassigned work
  2. NoticedFINDING: open → decided (per project or per flow stage)leaves only by a recorded disposition
  3. SignalCONDITIONstatus signal; only muted
  4. Muted / Snoozedmuted / snoozedpersonal, not a record act

Where the canon and the requirements disagree

Places where a design document and the approved requirements said different things, and where each stands now. A ruling folded into the canon wins; until the requirements carry it, nothing is built on it.

WhatThe canon saysThe requirements sayWhere it standsSource
